# Atlas V1 Execution Plan

> **Status:** CANONICAL EXECUTION AUTHORITY
> **Owner adopted:** 2026-07-15
> **Current active milestone:** ATLAS RECOVERY CAMPAIGN — Phase 4 (The canonical proof). Phase 3 (Shadow the packet and the trace) COMPLETE 2026-07-23 — closed on production evidence from Flight Recorder session FR-20260723120852-hw56ws9y; final divergence summary at `docs/verification/PHASE_3_DIVERGENCE_SUMMARY_2026-07-23.md`. Phase 2 (Inventory, contracts, and clean paper) COMPLETE 2026-07-21. Phase 1 (Soul Recovery, Issue #1073) CLOSED 2026-07-20 — the owner Soul gate PASSED, so the Phases 2–7 freeze is lifted; the recovery campaign remains the active work through Phase 7 (see the "Active campaign: Atlas Recovery Campaign" section below).
> **Rule:** Atlas has one execution plan. This document selects and sequences work until V1 is declared and the stabilization period ends. An owner instruction governs only once it is recorded here; the Atlas Recovery Campaign lives INSIDE this plan and is not a second roadmap.

## 1. Purpose

This document replaces the former active-roadmap, Soul, Soul-readiness, Post-Soul, remediation, and historical product-plan queues as execution authority.

Atlas is no longer choosing among overlapping plans. The governed path is:

> **Close Soul → harden trust seams → prove the whole product → complete five clean live sessions → declare V1 → stabilize → simplify the UI from observed use.**

The repository, not a Claude conversation, remembers campaign state. A fresh implementation session reads this file, selects the first eligible unfinished card, verifies current state, and continues from current `main`.

## 2. Authority and document roles

Read in this order for routine work:

1. `CLAUDE.md` — operating, safety, branch, review, and merge rules.
2. `docs/ATLAS_V1_EXECUTION_PLAN.md` — the sole work-selection and sequencing authority.
3. `docs/ATLAS_SYSTEM_AUTHORITY.md` — the ownership authority: what decides each concept today, what is intended to, and the exact sunset condition for every competing path.
4. `docs/DECISION_KERNEL.md` — durable product and trust principles.
5. `BACKLOG.md` — intake/deferred ledger and supporting finding detail; **not a competing queue** while this plan has eligible work.
6. Relevant specs, invariants, tests, and evidence ledgers.

The following remain separate because they are not execution plans:

- `docs/ATLAS_SYSTEM_AUTHORITY.md` — records authority; selects no work. It replaced `docs/ATLAS_OWNERSHIP_CONNECTIVITY_INVENTORY.md`, which was removed.
- `docs/ATLAS_PRODUCT_VISION.md` — product north star.
- `docs/CONSTITUTION.md` and `docs/INVARIANTS.md` — non-negotiable rules.
- `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` — current system boundaries.
- `docs/TEST_QUEUE.md` — owner/live evidence ledger.
- `docs/ONE_BRAIN_PROMOTION_CRITERIA.md` — reusable evidence standard for Brain promotion.
- `docs/BUG_TRIAGE_LEDGER.md` — Bug_Reports done/open record.
- Narrow design/spec/research docs — consulted only when the active card touches their surface.

If another document appears to select or sequence work, this plan wins and the conflicting document must be corrected in the same focused governance PR.

## 3. Execution contract

Claude works this plan as a durable campaign controller.

For every implementation card:

1. Refresh and verify current `main` and deployed version where applicable.
2. Run the Current-State Verification Gate and record exactly one verdict:
   `STILL BROKEN`, `ALREADY FIXED`, `PARTIALLY FIXED`, `FIXED BUT UNTESTED`, `STALE / SUPERSEDED`, or `NEEDS OWNER APP-TEST`.
3. If already fixed, do not manufacture code. Add the missing proof/status only.
4. Create one fresh `claude/<concern>` or `agent/<concern>` branch from current `main`.
5. Implement one concern only, with the smallest safe diff and live-path or closest-integration tests.
6. Run deterministic GitHub hard gates; address Codex advisory findings that are real and in scope.
7. Merge the exact passing head under Claude's standing authority unless a genuine owner-reserved data-safety category is involved.
8. Update this card's status and merged PR/commit as part of the card's PR when practical; otherwise make the smallest immediate status-only follow-up.
9. Refresh `main` and select the next eligible unfinished card.

### Stop conditions

Stop only for:

- a real production write requiring explicit authorization;
- schema, migration, deletion, credentials, or security-sensitive infrastructure;
- a Constitution/Invariant amendment;
- genuine owner-only gym/device evidence;
- the explicit One-Brain promotion decision;
- a truly non-derivable product conflict.

Do **not** stop merely because a PR is merge-ready, a session is getting long, or the next card is in a different milestone. Repository state is the handoff.

**STAGE B — WORKOUT 1 MISS (2026-08-01). Streak stays 0/5.** Owner verdict: **not yet**. Two owner-reported failures: (1) Atlas did not offer a substitute for bench press when asked; (2) it appeared not to save the workout.

**The workout WAS saved. The owner's training data is intact.** `npm run atlas:review-live` correlates **20 `Log_Cleaned` rows** to Flight Recorder session `FR-20260801152350-nngk9o6f` (15:23:50→15:34:27Z), matching the on-screen "5 exercises · 20 sets", with "Write verified (Log + Effort)" PASS and zero 5xx. 0 `Effort` rows is expected — the owner did not enter Apple Watch effort. What actually failed is the **plan-ledger seal**, and the client said so: *"Workout written to Google Sheets ✓ — but the plan-ledger record could not be verified. Your sets are safe."*

**Findings, classified:**

- **F-SB1-A — plan-ledger seal unverified (correctness).** `closeout_fully_verified === false` (`src/app/app.js`) with **0 `Session_Plan_Sets` rows** correlated against **2 `Session_Plans` rows**. The `Session_Plan_Sets` tab **exists** in the production workbook (verified read-only), so a missing tab is **excluded**. The condition fires when the ledger seal failed, a planned session had no ledger rows to bind, or the closeout event could not be recorded; the zero-checkpoint-rows evidence points at the middle branch. **Root cause NOT established.**
- **F-SB1-B — stuck Save control (trust/UX).** The recorded owner screenshot shows the button reading **"Saving…"**, but the `closeoutSealUnverified` branch sets it to **"Retry ledger seal"** and re-enables it. The promised retry was therefore not reachable as designed, which is why the owner read a successful write as a lost workout. A product that tells a trusting owner his workout vanished when it did not is a trust defect in its own right, independent of the ledger.
- **F-SB1-C — bench substitution not offered (coaching path). ROOT CAUSE ESTABLISHED 2026-08-01; FIXED.** The owner asked twice and **neither request reached the substitution lane**. Flight Recorder `FR-20260801152350-nngk9o6f` carries both turns and both misroutings.
  - **Turn 1, 15:28:44Z** — *"Bench press is taken at the moment, suggest a substitute workout out with reps and weights."* The composer gate `looksLikeSessionRequest` (`src/app/app.js`) claimed it: its loose phrase match `(suggest|recommend) (a |an |me )?(\w+ )?(workout|session)` has an optional adjective slot that took **"substitute"**. The turn was routed to the session-generation pick, and the recorder shows the only call it made was an idle `POST /api/coach/message` — the owner got today's plan read back at him (*"Today, let's make it core."*, painted 12×). No `/api/parse-workout-text`, no `/api/suggest-substitute`.
  - **Turn 2, 15:29:04Z** — *"No I'm looking for a substitute for bench press."* `classifyMutationIntent` (`src/app/planMutationIntent.js`) returned **`{action:'skip', target:"i'm looking for a substitute for bench press"}`**. The negation-style skip lane `^no\s+(?:more\s+)?(.+)$` captured the whole clause, and `looksLikeExercise` accepts any 2–60 character string, so the sentence became the skip target and resolved to the Bench Press slot. The recorder shows `POST /api/session-plans/outcome → 200` and the rendered line *"Skipped Bench Press. Next up: Dumbbell Side Bend."* **He asked for a swap and the plan silently dropped the lift.** The `POST /api/log-modality → 422` on the same turn is the modality probe falling through as designed — harmless, and not the cause.
  - **Fix.** Three parts. (1) The generation gate refuses a **lift-level** swap — a substitution word plus a named target or an unavailability report. A whole-session alternative ("recommend an alternative workout") names no lift and keeps its lane. (2) The negation-skip lane refuses a remainder opening with a subject pronoun or a discourse word ("no **I'm**…", "no **that's**…"), since that lane exists for a bare noun phrase ("no RDLs for me today"). (3) `classifyMutationIntent` gained a positive lane: "**\<substitute noun\> for|to X**" is an implicit substitution ON X. Turn 2 now returns `{action:'substitute', target:'bench press', implicit:true}` and reaches `tryApplyImplicitSubstitution`, which sends `intent:'substitute'` and gets a **deterministic** recommendation.
  - **Why part 3 was required (Codex P1, PR #1238).** Merely refusing to classify turn 2 was **not a fix**. It would have stopped the destructive skip but left the turn falling through to `checkAndSuggestSubstitute`, which sends no `intent` — so `/api/suggest-substitute`'s constraint gate ("busy"/"taken") refuses a message carrying no availability keyword, and the owner still gets generic coach prose instead of a swap. Turn 1 was unaffected because it says "taken". The lane also corrects the reading of "substitute **for** X", which previously treated the named lift as the *replacement* rather than the thing replaced.
  - Neither change touches the preview→approve→write loop.

**EVIDENCE CONTAMINANT — agent-caused, recorded against the agent.** Render redeployed **during** the workout: the review reports a build change `v146 → 9c9389299517… → d1dc225b3e60…`, and `d1dc225` is PR #1235, merged at **15:31:33Z**, inside the 15:23:50→15:34:27Z session window. That merge was mine and nothing required it then. Whether it contributed to F-SB1-A is **unknown and untested** — it is recorded as a contaminant, not a cause. It is the same split-build caveat that muddied LT-012, and it must not recur.

**RULE (governing, adopted 2026-08-01).** **No agent merges, deploys, or Render changes while a Stage B workout is in progress or its verdict is outstanding.** Stage B needs clean single-build owner evidence. Batch agent work between sessions; if a merge is genuinely urgent mid-session, it waits.

**F-SB1-A and F-SB1-B — FIXED (PR #1237).** The client collapsed three different `closeout_fully_verified:false` situations into one and reported the worst. A seal that **succeeded with nothing to seal** (`sealed_ok:true`, `no_ledger:true`, `reason:'no_rows'`) can never be cleared by tapping Save again, so the retry it offered was unreachable by construction. The save now completes and the negative verdict is reported as a persistent note under it — the verdict is never upgraded to verified, and a genuine seal failure keeps the retry path byte-identical. F-SB1-B was still live and separate: `app.js` relabels `#approve-btn` to "Retry ledger seal", but the owner only ever sees the review card's Save, which its own click handler had left reading "Saving…" — exactly what his screenshot showed. The card now mirrors that label. Both carry live browser proof (**F10D-NL** new, **F10D-RT** extended), and both are mutation-proven: removing either fix fails its spec.

**Why zero checkpoint rows — still NOT established.** PR #1237 fixes how the condition is *reported*; it does not explain why a planned session produced zero `Session_Plan_Sets` rows with the live-write flag on and the tab present. Whether the checkpoint ran while the flag was still rolling out (a Render env change itself redeploys, so the flag's own rollout may overlap the session start) remains untested. Stage B workout 1's rerun is the next evidence.

**F-SB1-C: substitution misrouting.** Diagnosed and fixed above (PR #1238). F-SB1 is closed.

## OWNER INSTRUCTION 2026-08-07 — SUPABASE HOT-PATH MIGRATION

**This instruction is the owner authorization. It governs because it is recorded here, not because it was said in a session.** Dale instructs that Atlas migrates its workout hot path from Google Sheets to Supabase.

**It supersedes further attempts to make Google Sheets satisfy the live workout read budget.** The read-quota work of PR #1271 and PR #1272 stands as merged code and as an honest measurement. It is no longer the path. Do not open another PR whose purpose is to fit a session inside the Google Sheets read quota.

**What this instruction does not do.** It changes no counter and no phase. **Rehearsal (F-SB4): 0/5 · Stage A: 5/5 COMPLETE · Stage B: 0/5 OPEN.** Phase 5 stays unauthorized. `SESSION_PLAN_SETS_WRITE_ENABLED` stays `0`. It authorizes no product-behaviour change, no schema application, and no deployment. It is not a second campaign, roadmap, or plan — it is one insertion inside this plan, executed as one closed chain of four PRs plus the one bounded post-window cleanup PR ruling D8 approved.

### Architecture ruling

- **Current authority.** Google Sheets is the runtime and permanent record for all workout data, plus the file-backed store in `services/idempotency.js` for write receipts.
- **Intended sole authority.** Supabase, for exactly seven concepts: workout sessions; logged sets; Effort; accepted session plans; session plan sets and revisions; item outcomes; closeout and write receipts.
- **Google Sheets becomes**, for those seven concepts only: a human-readable export and mirror, never required for an active workout to read, save, verify, or close out. Every other tab is unchanged, and Sheets stays the **editing** authority for `Exercise_Catalog`.
- **Competing authority to remove.** The direct Google Sheets runtime reads and writes for the seven migrated concepts, and the file-backed receipt store.
- **Temporary bridge.** A bounded shadow write, a durable divergence record, a reconciliation sweep, and a repair worker. The athlete-facing write succeeds or fails from the **current authority only**. Atlas never reconciles two authorities silently. Every bridge carries an exact sunset condition.

**Classification: authority defect.** The standing rule applies — select one winner, remove the loser, add no permanent reconciliation logic.

### The design document

[`docs/SUPABASE_HOT_PATH_MIGRATION.md`](SUPABASE_HOT_PATH_MIGRATION.md) holds the minimum schema, the field-by-field Sheet-column mapping, the closure chain — four PRs plus the one bounded post-window cleanup PR approved by ruling D8 — the exact proof required before each cutover, the failure and rollback rules, the security design, and the data-ownership record. It is a design specification. It selects no work; this block does.

### ATLAS CONTRACT / SYSTEMS REVIEW 2026-08-07 — exact head `b38de8b8e7da55f4e28b1c71ebe1bae97b5ca710`: BLOCKING

The required Atlas Contract / Systems Review read the exact head and returned **BLOCKING**, approving the direction and refusing that head. Four P1 architecture defects could still have produced a false-safe cutover, and one current-state statement was stale. All are corrected in the design document, and the rulings below govern.

1. **The durable divergence authority did not exist in the schema.** `S2` required a divergence record, `S3` required its count to reach zero, and `S4` gated on it — but §3 declared six tables and defined no such record, and `write_receipts` cannot carry one. **A required gate cannot depend on an unnamed record.** Corrected: `atlas.migration_divergences` is declared as an explicitly temporary migration-control table, dropped by `S4`.
2. **`S2` could lose both the shadow write and the evidence that it was missed.** A process death after the Sheets write and before the shadow write left Supabase short of rows while the open-divergence count still read zero. Corrected: the **reconciliation sweep** — not the inline record — is the completeness authority, because Sheets holds every committed row and the sweep depends on nothing a dying process was meant to write. The phrase "safest available means" is withdrawn; it was neither an authority nor a proof.
3. **The Sheets export was durable but not idempotent.** The derived queue survived a crash but permitted a re-export after a death between the append and the acknowledgement, and two workers could claim one session. Corrected: a claim lease plus an export identity key per table, with read-back-and-append-only-what-is-missing. **Superseded by the third review below:** the read-back-then-append step is replaced by a deterministic destination and `values.update`, which needs no read-back to decide what to write.
4. **The file-backed idempotency store carried two incompatible sunset rulings** — deleted by `S4` in the authority map, surviving `S4` in the design, and both in the ownership table. Corrected by ruling D4 below.

**Current-state correction.** D3 is no longer open. The owner created and selected the Free-tier project **Atlas Production**, independently verified healthy and empty, `us-west-2`, zero public tables, zero migrations. Any statement that no project was provisioned, or that its state is unknown, is stale. The project reference and every credential stay out of the repository.

### ATLAS CONTRACT / SYSTEMS REVIEW 2026-08-07 (second) — exact head `5f42d3c42993b8ae295bf711655a2f0448f5ec9d`: BLOCKING

The four blockers above were confirmed closed and rulings D1–D5 confirmed applied consistently. That head still could not pass: **the replacement design introduced four new load-bearing gaps.** All four are corrected in the design document.

1. **The `Exercise_Catalog` mirror had no freshness authority.** The design said a failed sync leaves the previous mirror in place, and called that the same behaviour as the existing 60-second cache. It is not: `sheets.js:738-739` drops the cache entry at expiry — *"Explicit expiry: drop it here so no path below can fall back to it"* — and a failed refresh throws. The live path is **fail-closed**; the proposed mirror was **fail-open** and could enrich Saves indefinitely from content that silently disagreed with the Sheets editing authority. Corrected: a separate `atlas.exercise_catalog_sync` freshness authority, a `CATALOG_MIRROR_MAX_AGE` bound past which the Save **fails closed**, a failed sync that never advances currency, refusal of an empty or materially shrunken source, and a catalog content mismatch that opens a divergence.
2. **The export lease was not a fencing mechanism.** `export_claim_token` guarded only the Supabase acknowledgement, and a Google Sheets append cannot check a Supabase token. Once worker A's lease expired — slow, timed out, or still in flight — worker B could claim, read the rows as missing, and append them too. A could no longer acknowledge but **could still write**. Corrected: a **session-level Postgres advisory lock** serialises read-back, append, verify and acknowledgement. It is not time-based, so no two-holders window exists, and the database releases it when the holding connection dies. The token is narrowed to an acknowledgement guard and may never again be described as mutual exclusion. **Partly superseded by the third review below:** the advisory lock does not fence an in-flight external append either, and correctness now rests on a deterministic destination rather than on any lock.
3. **The stated security model could not perform the design.** It specified one service-role key **and** separate least-privilege roles **and** `DELETE` on `logged_sets` only — while the catalog swap needs `DELETE` on the mirror. One key does not authenticate as three database roles. Corrected: **direct Postgres connections with three explicitly scoped roles** (`atlas_app`, `atlas_migrate`, `atlas_readonly`), no Data API, no service-role key and no anon key in the runtime path, and a grant table that matches what the design actually does. Least privilege is now proven by a test, not claimed.
4. **`S2` child rows referenced a receipt row `S2` never created.** `logged_sets.write_id` and `session_effort.write_id` reference `atlas.write_receipts`, but ruling D4 moves the receipt authority at `S4`. Every `S2` shadow Save would have violated the foreign key or silently dropped the `write_id`. Corrected at the time by mirroring the decided receipt parent-first inside the shadow transaction. **Superseded by the third review below:** that mirror could not be reconstructed after a process death, because Sheets stores no `write_id`. The receipt is no longer mirrored during `S2`/`S3` at all; the child column is nullable with no foreign key until `S4`.

**No counter changed and no authorization was granted by this review.** Campaign 0/5, Phase 5 unauthorized, no schema applied, no deployment.

### ATLAS CONTRACT / SYSTEMS REVIEW 2026-08-07 (third) — exact head `2ce7be3b31e7ff3ab7a25bbe4bcf98bd1e5cdc39`: BLOCKING

The second review's four defects were confirmed materially addressed and all CI on that head was green. **Five load-bearing gaps remained**, four of them in mechanisms the previous corrections introduced. All five are corrected in the design document.

1. **The advisory lock still could not fence a late external append.** Postgres releases the lock the moment the holding connection drops — while the Google Sheets request is still in flight. Worker B then acquires the lock, reads the rows as missing, and appends; worker A's already-sent request commits later. The repository already establishes that an ambiguous append may have committed: `isTransientAppendError` (`sheets.js:52-76`) refuses to retry a non-429 failure for exactly that reason. So the claim that a timed-out worker "abandons without appending rather than appending late" was **not a property the client can guarantee**. Corrected: the mirror write becomes **idempotent by destination**. `sheets_row_start` is allocated once per session per tab in the claiming transaction, and the export uses `values.update` into that exact range instead of `values.append`. A late duplicate overwrites its own identical values in its own cells and cannot create a second copy. The lock is kept for throughput only and may never be called fencing again. **Partly superseded by the fourth review below:** allocating the destination per session did not serialise *different* sessions, so the allocation authority moved to a per-tab cursor with an exclusion constraint.
2. **The receipt sweep could not reconstruct a receipt lost in the `S2` death window.** `Log_Cleaned` and `Effort` carry **no `write_id` column**, so Sheets can prove the child rows exist but cannot reveal the missing receipt's identity, and the `/tmp` file store may be gone after the same restart. The parent-first repair was therefore not implementable from the declared completeness authority — the first review's process-death defect, one level down. Corrected by **removing receipt mirroring from `S2`/`S3` entirely**: child `write_id` is nullable with **no foreign key until `S4`**, `write_receipts` is not a sweep concept, a repaired row carries `write_id = NULL` rather than a fabricated value, and `S4` adds the foreign key in the same step that makes Supabase the receipt authority.
3. **`S2` had no `workout_sessions` parent for any shadow child.** `S2` shadows against an empty schema and the backfill is `S3`, so the first shadow write for a real session would have violated the session foreign key. Corrected: every shadow transaction inserts or confirms its `workout_sessions` parent first, in the same transaction. The parent is derivable by parsing `session_id`, so no extra source is needed.
4. **`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` could not mirror retryable receipt transitions.** A `failed` outcome mirrored first would have discarded a later `completed` retry, and out-of-order work had no version to adjudicate. Corrected by correction 2 — with nothing mirrored, there is no terminal state to overwrite.
5. **The quota acceptance gate still claimed more than the design provides.** §1.1 called P4 an unqualified true statement and P4 was still titled "No athlete-facing dependency on the Sheets quota" with an expected residual of zero, while §3.7 correctly said the dependency is bounded, not eliminated. A replay can prove zero in-request reads; it cannot prove Save availability is independent of the Sheets quota across the mirror-age window. Corrected: P4 is renamed to **"No in-request Sheets read on the migrated Save path, plus a measured and bounded background dependency"**, and it now gates on sync interval, `CATALOG_MIRROR_MAX_AGE`, fail-closed behaviour, and the exact residual. Unqualified quota independence may not be certified.

**No counter changed and no authorization was granted by this review.** Campaign 0/5, Phase 5 unauthorized, no schema applied, no deployment.

### ATLAS CONTRACT / SYSTEMS REVIEW 2026-08-07 (fourth) — exact head `0878f6164378cc7a17784934451a244ec63d347d`: BLOCKING

The third review's five findings were confirmed closed, and the replacement design confirmed materially better. **Four load-bearing gaps remained**, plus five documentation corrections. All are addressed in the design document.

1. **`sheets_row_start` had no collision-free authority across DIFFERENT sessions.** The design proved only that two workers claiming the *same* session read the same value. Two different sessions could inspect the same tail, allocate the same start row, and `values.update` would then have **one session overwrite the other** — silent loss of an athlete's workout from the mirror. A single transactional statement over two different `workout_sessions` rows serialises nothing, and the session-level advisory lock deliberately does not serialise different sessions. Corrected: the destination authority moves to `atlas.sheets_mirror_cursor` (one row per tab, atomic fetch-and-add) plus `atlas.sheets_mirror_allocations` (PK `(tab, session_id)` preserving allocations across retries, with an **`EXCLUDE USING gist`** constraint that makes two overlapping blocks on one tab structurally unrepresentable). Allocation covers every tab in one transaction, so a failure reserves nothing.
2. **The `S4` foreign keys could not be added over the `S2`/`S3` data described.** The design let a shadow row record `write_id` "when it happens to have one" while never mirroring a receipt, so those non-null values had no parent and the `S4` constraint would have failed validation before the receipt authority could start. Corrected to the smallest coherent rule: **`S2`/`S3` always store `write_id = NULL`**; `S4` adds the foreign key; only post-cutover writes carry a non-null value backed by a receipt created in the same authority path.
3. **"Reads and writes move together" was a desired result, not an executable protocol.** A merge plus a deploy is a rolling replacement, so an old instance could complete an acknowledged Sheets write after the final `S3` sweep while a new instance already read and wrote Supabase — recreating the acknowledged-write omission the migration exists to remove, and making the export's initial per-tab base unsafe. Corrected: §5.5 defines the eight-step handover — freeze (shipped as its own **earlier** deploy so old instances honour it), drain with a positive proof, final sweep to zero divergences, record the per-tab base, apply the schema and receipt transition, deploy the cutover, run the non-counting proof workout, and only then reopen writes — with an abort path and a tail re-verification before reopening.
4. **Verification read only the allocated range while claiming to detect duplicates anywhere.** A duplicate outside the block was invisible, so a session could be marked exported while another copy of the same identity existed elsewhere in the tab. Corrected: the verifier reads **the whole relevant tab** and asserts exactly one row per identity across it, with the extra cost stated rather than hidden.

**Documentation corrections applied:** P14/P14a now name `values.update` rather than "append"; `workout_sessions` states exactly four mutable export columns and points at §3.9 for the destination; `S3` names the tables the workbook can actually source and records that `write_receipts` is deliberately not backfilled; D1 in §9 adopts the bounded / in-request wording P4 uses; and the PR merge card is rewritten to the current head and current mechanisms.

New proof gates: **P14b** (concurrent different-session allocation is disjoint; a failed allocation reserves nothing; the exclusion constraint bites), **P14c** (a duplicate seeded outside the allocated range refuses acknowledgement), **P19** (the handover protocol, with an old-authority write refused or detected before writes reopen), **P20** (the foreign keys validate against genuine shadow data, not an empty database).

**No counter changed and no authorization was granted by this review.** Campaign 0/5, Phase 5 unauthorized, no schema applied, no deployment.

### ADVISORY REVIEW 2026-08-07 (fifth) — clean-context review of `7057b31b12b1406acd2c8994b22cf560453bed63`

**This is ADVISORY, not the required gate.** `CLAUDE.md` is explicit: ChatGPT performs the required Atlas Contract / Systems Review, and a clean-context review by the implementation agent "never satisfies the required gate". This entry is recorded under advisory findings. The four BLOCKING reviews above came from the owner and did satisfy the gate; **`7057b31` still awaits its required review.**

**Scope limit, stated rather than worked around.** PR S1 contains **zero code** — eight markdown files, no `.js`, no `.sql`, no migration, no adapter, no test. The allocator, cursor, exclusion constraint, freeze, drain, verifier and twelve tables exist **only as specification**. Concurrency, crash, deadlock, constraint-validation and failure-injection properties therefore **cannot be executed against them**, and no such execution is claimed. What was verified: every code citation the design makes about **existing production code**, and the specification's internal consistency attacked at mechanism level.

**Citation audit — all correct.** `sheets.js:738-739` (explicit cache expiry, no stale fallback), `sheets.js:754-756` (empty catalog never cached), `sheets.js:52-76` (ambiguous append non-retryable), `services/idempotency.js:18` and `:178-181`, the seven `beginWrite` call sites, `config/columns.js` column counts and the absence of `write_id`, `getLogCompositeKeys` producing `sid||ex||setn` fully lower-cased, `services/sessionId.js`, `services/sessionPlanEvents.js`, `services/sessionPlanLedger.js`, `services/closeoutFinality.js`. No miscitation found; the design is not built on a false premise about existing code.

**Three specification defects found, all corrected in the design.**

1. **P1 — row-position stability was assumed, never established, and the failure was detected only after destruction.** The allocator persists `start_row` **durably and indefinitely**; correctness then depends on absolute row positions never drifting. Atlas makes no such assumption today: `sessionPlanSetsStore.sealCloseout` — the one place production writes by position — **re-derives** positions from a fresh read inside a single operation (`sheet row = i + 2`) and never persists one. Three drift paths were open: `deleteRowsByRange` issues `deleteDimension` (`sheets.js:970-993`), which **shifts** every row below it; an owner edit to a workbook the owner already edits by hand; and any structural change. The whole-tab verifier could not catch it — a shift creates no duplicate — so the next export would have **overwritten another session's workout**, discovered only afterwards. Corrected: the export **verifies its allocated range immediately before writing** and refuses on anything that is not blank or its own identities, opening a `mirror_range_occupied` divergence. The check is free — the whole-tab read already covers the range. Recorded in design §5.4 and §5.6.
2. **P1 — undo had no defined effect on the mirror.** Post-cutover undo retracts in Supabase only. Either `deleteRowsByRange` stayed live on a mirrored tab and silently invalidated every allocation above it, or an undone session's sets remained in the human-readable record permanently — a false record of the athlete's training. Corrected: `deleteRowsByRange` is deleted for every mirrored tab at `S4` and added to the deletion list; undo marks the session for re-export; the rewrite stays **inside the session's own allocated block**, blanking its tail, so the cursor never moves backwards and no other session is touched.
3. **P2 — no re-export path after any post-export change.** `sheets_exported_at` was set once with nothing to unset it, and the derived queue only finds `IS NULL`, so a session mutated after export stayed permanently stale — reachable by undo and by the `closeout_write_id` seal, the one mutable column. Corrected by **reusing the existing queue**: any mutation of exported data sets `sheets_exported_at = NULL` and the session re-enters by the same predicate. No dirty flag, no outbox, no new table.

Also corrected: allocation must cover **only the tabs a session actually has rows for**, since a session with no `Effort` row is routine and would otherwise strand blank reserved rows and advance that tab's cursor for nothing.

**A second pass over the receipt surface found three more gaps, all corrected.** (1) **`peekWrite` was missing from the specification entirely** — it is live at `index.js:2511`, recovers a prior attempt's server-minted `session_id`, and would have been left without a replacement. The adapter surface is four operations, not three. (2) **The 24-hour TTL had no Postgres mechanism** — the file store prunes "on load" and Postgres has no load, so the rule is now a read predicate plus a periodic delete. (3) **A documented safety property is deliberately reversed at `S4`** — today *"a disk failure NEVER fails a workout write"* (`services/idempotency.js:16-18`); after cutover the receipt is written inside the Save's own transaction, so a receipt failure fails the Save closed. That is the safer behaviour and it is intended, but it is a reversal and is now recorded rather than left to happen quietly. (4) **Deleting `test/idempotencyPersistence.test.js` with the store would drop live proofs**: five of its eleven guarantees are authority guarantees the new store still owes, and they must be re-proven against Supabase before that suite is deleted.

New proof gates **P14d–P14g**, **P16a**, **P16b**. Net new schema: **zero** — every correction reuses existing structure, and one production capability (`deleteRowsByRange` on mirrored tabs) is scheduled for deletion.

**Convergence note, for the owner.** The deterministic-destination export has now failed adversarial review twice on the same property — concurrent overlap (fourth review) and positional drift (this one) — each time through a different door. The corrections are sound, but the pattern is worth weighing at the next required review: this mechanism buys idempotency at the cost of an invariant Sheets does not naturally provide.

**No counter changed and no authorization was granted by this review.** Campaign 0/5, Phase 5 unauthorized, no schema applied, no deployment.

### ATLAS CONTRACT / SYSTEMS REVIEW 2026-08-07 (fifth required) — exact head `771ff83df6d489a25395e1d8fba860776e16be18`: BLOCKING

The owner confirmed the PR is documentation-only, the head exact, CI green, and **the overnight advisory findings real — they are not reopened**. Four further P1 gaps, all corrected.

1. **The connection model was not executable on this deployment.** §8.1 required **direct Postgres**, but Supabase's direct endpoint is **IPv6** on Free and Supabase's own troubleshooting page lists **Render** as IPv4-only; the IPv4 add-on is paid and conflicts with the Free-tier ruling. **Owner ruling: the runtime connects through Supavisor SESSION MODE (port 5432)** — not direct, and **not transaction mode**, because the exporter's `pg_try_advisory_lock` is **session-scoped** and transaction mode would take it on a connection the next statement may not use. It would appear to work and hold nothing. Migration and read-only tooling may use the direct endpoint only where IPv6 reachability is **proven**; CI (also IPv4-only) uses the pooler. The three scoped roles survive pooling — Supavisor authenticates the role through the pooler username — and each gets its own connection string. **No paid IPv4 add-on.** New gate P8b proves a real session-mode connection opens a transaction and holds a session-level advisory lock across statements.
2. **The permanent exporter wrote to an authority `S4` deletes.** `mirror_range_occupied` and whole-tab duplicate detection opened `migration_divergences` rows — but that table, its sweep and its repair worker are **temporary and dropped at `S4`**, leaving a permanent mechanism writing to a table that no longer exists, with no consumer. **Ruling: the divergence table stays temporary and no permanent divergence subsystem is kept alive to serve the mirror.** Post-cutover mirror failures are session-addressable **mirror state** — `sheets_export_error` plus an unacknowledged `sheets_exported_at`, surfaced by `atlas:status`. **The exporter fails its own acknowledgement, never the workout.** No post-`S4` path depends on `migration_divergences`.
3. **The receipt TTL did not preserve current retry semantics.** The new predicate filtered on `created_at`, which the `failed → in_progress` and stale-`in_progress` transitions never refreshed — while the file store starts a **clean record** on retry with a fresh lifetime. A write first attempted at 23h59m could retry successfully and then vanish from `peekWrite` and duplicate replay a minute later. Corrected: an explicit **`expires_at`** is the single TTL winner, refreshed to `now() + 24 hours` on every newly-owned attempt; `created_at` becomes immutable provenance that decides nothing. New boundary gate P8a.
4. **Deterministic destinations survive, but the mirror contract changes and drift needs a recovery path.** The owner **kept the mechanism** — it is the first in this chain that makes the cross-system late-write race idempotent without pretending a Postgres lock can fence Google Sheets — and **rejected the advisory sentence that "a mirror nobody may touch is not a human-readable mirror". Human-readable does not mean human-editable**; that sentence is withdrawn. After cutover the four migrated tabs are **generated export surfaces**, Supabase is the **sole editing authority** for migrated workout data, `Exercise_Catalog` stays Sheets-edited under D1, and structural inserts and deletes on the generated tabs are **unsupported** — with Atlas itself forbidden from shifting rows there. The contract joins the D2 Constitution amendment. The pre-write range check remains a fail-safe for accidental drift, but refusal alone was a **permanent stall** — allocations are immutable, the cursor never moves backwards, and the repair worker is gone by then. New **§5.7**: one owner-only **mirror rebuild** that pauses export, reconstructs the four tabs from Supabase, reissues allocations and cursors past the new tail, verifies the whole projection, then resumes — and never touches Supabase workout authority. New gate P14h proves drift reaches refusal and recovers through exactly that procedure.

**No counter changed and no authorization was granted by this review.** Campaign 0/5, Phase 5 unauthorized, no schema applied, no deployment.

### ATLAS CONTRACT / SYSTEMS REVIEW 2026-08-07 (sixth required) — exact head `0e324ac9b34d89acdacb2472727fec8c2df7e0ee`: BLOCKING

Current-state gate passed: PR open, mergeable, documentation-only, base still `862a23b5`, exact-head checks green. Review 5's four findings confirmed materially addressed, and **the exporter architecture is explicitly not reopened** — deterministic destinations plus generated/read-only migrated tabs remain the selected design. Four P1 specification defects remained; all corrected.

1. **`write_receipts.expires_at` was not an executable state machine — three holes in one mechanism.** (a) The initial claim never inserted `expires_at` and the column had no default, so **every new receipt carried a null TTL authority**: `peekWrite` filtered it out immediately and the prune never owned it. A shield invisible the instant it is created is not a shield. (b) The `atlas_app` grant list omitted `expires_at` while the retry claim updated it — **permission-denied under its own security model**. (c) The file store calls `pruneExpired()` **synchronously before every `beginWrite`**, so an expired record is gone before the claim; filtering on read while deleting on a timer is not equivalent. An expired `completed` row still held the primary key, `ON CONFLICT` saw it, the retry `WHERE` refused it as completed, and the TTL-filtered read reported it absent — **the `write_id` was wedged until housekeeping ran**. Corrected: expiry is part of the atomic claim. The insert sets it; every newly-owned attempt refreshes it; an expired row is **reclaimable in the same statement at any status**, resetting `attempt` and `created_at` to match prune-then-insert. The prune job survives demoted to size housekeeping and carries no correctness. Grants and gates P8a0/P8a updated to the actual SQL.
2. **The owner-only mirror rebuild had no principal able to execute it.** §5.7 requires clearing and reissuing `sheets_mirror_allocations` and resetting cursor metadata, but `atlas_app` holds no `DELETE` there, `atlas_migrate` is DDL-only, and `atlas_readonly` is `SELECT`-only. **A recovery path no credential may execute is not a recovery path.** Corrected: one new owner-only principal **`atlas_rebuild`** with exactly the required DML and nothing else — `SELECT` everywhere, `DELETE`/`INSERT` on `sheets_mirror_allocations`, column-scoped `UPDATE` on the cursor and the export state. It holds **no write grant on any workout table**, so "never touches Supabase workout authority" is enforced by the grant rather than by the procedure's good intentions. Its credential is **not** configured in the Render runtime. One role and one procedure — not a generic admin framework. P14h now exercises the real principal.
3. **Permanent mirror failures were durable but unboundedly retry-eligible.** The derived queue was `session_closeout` + `sheets_exported_at IS NULL`, and structural failures deliberately left that null — so a condition **only the owner rebuild can fix** stayed eligible on every worker pass, and **each pass performs the expensive whole-tab read**. That recreates the exact class of Sheets quota storm that caused this migration, and competes with the permanent `Exercise_Catalog` sync; starve that sync past `CATALOG_MIRROR_MAX_AGE` and **athlete Saves fail closed**. Corrected by classifying failures: **structural** (`mirror_range_occupied`, `mirror_duplicate_identity`) becomes `blocked`, leaves the queue, consumes **zero** further Sheets reads, and stays visible in `atlas:status` as owner action required; **transient** becomes `retry_backoff` on an explicit bounded, capped cadence, escalating to `blocked` at the attempt ceiling rather than retrying forever. Two columns on `workout_sessions`, no new table. Gate **P14i** measures it in Sheets reads, not intent.
4. **The post-`S4` catalog rule treated a legitimate owner edit as a failed sync.** D1 makes Sheets the **editing authority** for `Exercise_Catalog`, so a changed `content_hash` is the normal signal that the owner edited it — and ingesting it is the entire purpose of the swap defined immediately below the rule. As written, a routine catalog edit would have been recorded as a failure, would not have advanced currency, and would eventually have **503'd athlete Saves**. This defect was introduced by the previous round's correction. Corrected by separating **source changed** (normal — drives the transactional swap, produces a new verified generation, currency advances, nothing recorded as failure) from **sync failed** (read failure, empty or materially shrunken source, verification failure, swap-transaction failure — records `status='failed'`, prior generation stays current and ages). `S2`/`S3` parity mismatches may still open the temporary divergence; after `S4` normal edits converge through the permanent sync. Gate **P7b1** proves changing one valid row yields a new verified generation with the Save path continuously serviceable.

**No counter changed and no authorization was granted by this review.** Campaign 0/5, Phase 5 unauthorized, no schema applied, no deployment.

### ATLAS CONTRACT / SYSTEMS REVIEW 2026-08-07 (seventh required) — exact head `c22ce02d20ad23867d51bc3b092578a602d8835e`: BLOCKING

Current-state gate passed; the exporter architecture is again **not reopened**. Five P1 defects plus four consistency corrections; all addressed.

1. **`S4` discarded the live receipt authority instead of handing it over — an authority-transfer defect.** This document claimed "no receipt data to migrate — only a decider to change". **False as a cutover property.** Immediately before cutover the file store can hold up to **24 hours of unexpired live safety state**: completed replay records with response bodies, retryable `failed` records, fresh and rehydrated `in_progress` records, and the server-minted `session_id` WRITE-2 needs. Pointing seven callers at an empty table and deleting the store destroys it, so a **lost-response retry straddling the cutover looks brand new** — minting a second session identity for a server-minted workout, or permitting a second Sheets append for a D4 route where no Supabase constraint can catch it. The freeze also covered only the four migrated workout routes while D4 moves receipt authority for **all seven** callers, leaving four routes deciding duplicates against the old store while the new build decided against Supabase. New **§5.5a**: the freeze covers all seven; every unexpired receipt is carried into Supabase and **verified** before the new decider opens; **the old authority is deleted last**, as step 9, never before the carry-over is proven; and rollback is symmetric — after writes reopen, Supabase-only receipts are carried **back** before Sheets writes resume. Gates **P19a** (lost-response retry across cutover **and** rollback, for a server-minted workout and a non-workout D4 route) and **P19b**.
2. **The claim never persisted `session_id`, so WRITE-2 could not work.** The schema had the column and the text promised `peekWrite` would recover the server-minted id, but the claim SQL never wrote one — P16b could not have passed. The adapter now records it at the moment the server mints it, in its own token-guarded statement, so an obsolete attempt cannot overwrite a newer one. A **live retry preserves** it; an **expired reclaim clears** it. P16b now requires a non-null value and proves the overwrite guard.
3. **The stale-`in_progress` rule removed the restart/death condition.** The file store retries an aged `in_progress` record **only when it was rehydrated from a prior process** (`services/idempotency.js:159-177`); an in-process record may still be running and stays a duplicate. The SQL reclaimed **any** row older than five minutes, which can start a **second live attempt while the first is still running** — the token stops it acknowledging but not from performing **external effects**, and the four D4 routes still append to Sheets. Corrected: the claimer holds `pg_try_advisory_lock(hashtext(write_id))` for the life of the attempt, so a lock that **cannot be taken proves another attempt is genuinely live**, and a released lock is the actual "prior process died" evidence. The five-minute window is a secondary bound; **the lock is the authority**. P16a now requires WRITE-3 proven by that mechanism.
4. **The mirror rebuild had two recovery holes.** Its final step cleared only `sheets_export_error`, while a structural refusal is `sheets_export_state='blocked'` and the queue excludes blocked — so every repaired session stayed permanently unexportable, contradicting P14h's own claim that it returns to `queued`. And it rebuilt from **every** Supabase row while merely pausing the worker: an **open** workout would have been projected into an immutable allocation too small for its eventual closeout, and with no snapshot the target could move under the verification. Corrected: the pause is **proven** like §5.5's drain; the rebuild covers only the **closed-session projection** the exporter owns; it runs against a **fixed `REPEATABLE READ` snapshot**; and it resets the **whole export-state tuple** atomically.
5. **Two governing statements still contradicted ruling D5** — `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` and the plan's §5 supersession line both said Sheets stays authoritative "until PR S3 moves the reads". D5 is that **S3 moves nothing and S4 moves reads and writes together**. Both corrected; stale authority instructions in the PR whose purpose is to resolve authority.

**Consistency corrections applied:** `workout_sessions` now states **six** mutable export columns, not four; the transient retry policy is declared concretely (`least(2 ^ attempts, 60)` minutes — 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 60, 60 … — capped at 60, `blocked` at 8 attempts, no jitter, so P14i has an exact schedule to prove); the stale "opens a divergence" sentence in permanent export verification now names the permanent mirror state; and the merge card is updated to the exact head and review count.

**No counter changed and no authorization was granted by this review.** Campaign 0/5, Phase 5 unauthorized, no schema applied, no deployment.

### ATLAS CONTRACT / SYSTEMS REVIEW 2026-08-07 (eighth required) — exact head `60f27b3e29dce94f130e174fb732d9114d584ab9`: BLOCKING

Current-state gate passed; the exporter architecture is again **not reopened**. **Six** P1 defects (the review body's "five" was corrected to six by the owner in a follow-up), all in the replacement receipt-handover, liveness and rebuild mechanisms, plus two consistency corrections. All addressed.

1. **The receipt carry-over was not executable against the store that actually exists.** §5.5a filtered on `expires_at > now()` and preserved `attempt` and `response_body` — but the file store has **no `expires_at`**, **no `attempt`**, and names the payload **`response`**; its TTL authority is `created_at_ms + DEFAULT_TTL_MS`. An implementation following the design literally could not enumerate the records it was required to preserve. Corrected with an exact **forward and reverse** field mapping against the real persisted JSON: expiry derived from `created_at_ms`, `response → response_body`, `metadata.session_id → session_id`, `metadata.endpoint → route`, `attempt` fixed at 1 because the source has no counter, and process-local fields (`rehydrated`, `token`, `failed_at*`) dropped. P19a must use the **real** JSON shape, never a schema-shaped fixture. The contradictory sentence "There is no receipt data to migrate — only a decider to change" is **deleted** (it survives only as the quoted, withdrawn claim inside its own correction).
2. **WRITE-2 was impossible under the declared grants.** The token-guarded `UPDATE … SET session_id` was specified while `atlas_app`'s column-scoped grant omitted `session_id`. Grant added. **This is the third grant/SQL mismatch to reach review**, so P7c now requires **every** specified statement to be executed as its real role — a statement proven only as superuser proves nothing about the deployed system.
3. **WRITE-2's route ordering contradicted the live flow.** The live path is `peekWrite` → **mint** → work → `beginWrite` (`index.js:2506-2530`, `:2683`), so at mint time no Supabase attempt is owned and **no attempt token exists** to guard the update the design specified. One executable ordering is now specified, strictly stronger than today's: **claim first** (the claim `RETURNING`s `session_id`), completed-replay unaffected because the claim refuses a `completed` row and never reaches the mint, then reuse-or-mint, then persist under the token before the workout write. Recorded plainly as a **behaviour change at `S4`, not a storage swap**. P16b exercises the real ordering including death after mint-and-persist.
4. **The session-level receipt lock had no normal-release contract.** A session-scoped advisory lock does not end at commit, and returning a pooled client does not close the session. Acquisition and process-death release were specified; the ordinary path was not — so a normal request could leak a lock into the pool, falsely blocking a later request or, on the same backend session, letting **advisory-lock reentrancy** invert "lock acquired proves no other owner is live" into a false negative. Corrected with three rules: the connection is **pinned** across claim → external effect → complete/fail → unlock; `pg_advisory_unlock` runs **unconditionally in a `finally`** on success, failure, duplicate, refusal and thrown error; and reentrancy is why pinning is a requirement, not an optimisation. P16a proves all three.
5. **The rebuilt mirror state needed grants `atlas_rebuild` did not have.** §5.7 resets the whole tuple including `sheets_export_attempts` and `export_claim_token`, which were not in its grant list — the recovery procedure was permission-denied under its own principal for two of the six columns it promises to reset. Both added; P14h executes the complete reset as the real principal.
6. **Rollback after writes reopen had no reverse freeze/drain boundary.** Carrying receipts back without first re-freezing meant a new Supabase receipt could be created **after the reverse read and before the old build resumed**, vanishing from the restored file authority — the exact lost-response boundary this mechanism exists to close, reintroduced by the rollback. Rollback-after-open is now its own ordered transfer: **freeze all seven → positive drain → fixed snapshot → carry back → verify by identity and content → restore the old decider → reopen last.** P19a injects a receipt concurrent with the rollback and proves it cannot fall through.

**Consistency corrections applied:** §5.5's heading said "eight ordered steps" while the list contained `2a` and `9` — now honestly **ten**, so the runbook cannot omit a step by following its own heading. The PR merge card was stale (reporting `0e324ac`, five reviews, a sixth required) and is updated to the exact head and review count; **the implementation agent had stated in a PR comment that it was already updated, which was untrue** — recorded here because a false status claim to the owner is a trust defect, not a clerical one.

**No counter changed and no authorization was granted by this review.** Campaign 0/5, Phase 5 unauthorized, no schema applied, no deployment.

### ATLAS CONTRACT / SYSTEMS REVIEW 2026-08-07 (ninth required) — exact head `4647ee2cb6aab571a821c3a92d433f706a01b771`: BLOCKING

Current-state gate passed; review 8's six findings confirmed closed; the exporter architecture again **not reopened**. Six P1 authority/state-machine defects, all in the receipt handover. All corrected.

1. **The forward handover read the file, but the live receipt authority can be memory-only.** Persistence is **best-effort**: after a write failure the module sets `persistDisabled` (`services/idempotency.js:141`) and keeps serving from the in-memory `writeRecords` map (`:128-130`). The file can therefore be stale or missing while the live process still holds a completed replay record, a retryable `failed` record, or a server-minted `session_id` Atlas will honour — so a lost-response retry protected before cutover looks brand new after it, with a field-correct mapping hiding the loss. Corrected: the extraction authority is the **live in-memory map of the frozen old process**, with the persisted JSON used only when no live process remains and only with `persistDisabled` plus a disk-vs-map comparison recorded as evidence. P19a must inject the persistence-failure fallback; a disk-only fixture does not discharge it.
2. **The freeze deploy could destroy the `/tmp` source before the carry-over ran.** The protocol shipped the freeze as an earlier deploy and carried receipts afterwards — while this same document records that `/tmp/atlas-idempotency.json` may be gone after a restart. **The handover created its own authority-loss window**, and "the file remains the rollback source until step 9" is no help if step 1 removed it. Corrected: the freeze capability **ships dormant in `S3`'s already-deployed build** so activation needs no deploy; and if activation cannot avoid a restart on this platform — a Render env change itself redeploys — the live state is **durably captured and verified before the restart**, and that capture is the transfer source. New gate **P19c** injects loss of the old process and filesystem at the freeze boundary.
3. **The reverse mapping destroyed the refreshed TTL epoch.** §3.6 separates the clocks — `created_at` immutable provenance, `expires_at` refreshed per attempt — and the reverse mapping reconstructed the file's `created_at_ms` from Supabase `created_at`. A receipt first seen 23 hours ago and retried to completion now has an old `created_at` and a fresh 24-hour `expires_at`; rolling back two hours later wrote a `created_at_ms` **25 hours old**, so the file store **pruned on load a receipt Supabase still owed for 22 hours**, silently losing replay and WRITE-2 protection across rollback. Corrected: `created_at_ms` carries the **active lifetime clock** (`expires_at − 24h`, equivalently `attempt_started_at`). Provenance is discarded and costs nothing — the file store never had it.
4. **`failWrite` did not invalidate the token.** The live store sets `token: null` on failure precisely so a stale `completeWrite` cannot resurrect the released attempt (`services/idempotency.js:249-255`). The design omitted it, leaving legal: own token T → `failWrite(A,T)` → **before any retry replaces T** a late `completeWrite(A,T)` arrives → T matches → the failed attempt becomes `completed`. That contradicts P8's own promise. Corrected: `failWrite` sets `attempt_token = NULL` in the same guarded update, making the resurrection unrepresentable. New gate **P8c** is the direct bite.
5. **The frozen drain could wedge forever on the current store's lazy stale transition.** A rehydrated `in_progress` record is downgraded **only inside `beginWrite`** (`:165-177`); there is no timer. Freeze all seven routes and that call is unreachable, so a record four minutes old at the freeze becomes stale and stays physically `in_progress` **forever** — the declared "zero `in_progress`" is unreachable. Corrected: the frozen drain performs the transition itself as **step 0**, using the current authority's exact age/death semantics, and **nothing `in_progress` transfers** — which also makes the "decided outcomes" wording true rather than aspirational.
6. **Step 9 was not executable inside one immutable build.** Source deletion is not a runtime transition: one deployed commit cannot both retain the file store through steps 6–8 as the rollback source **and** have it absent at step 9. The runbook was hiding a second deploy inside a numbered step. Corrected at the time by splitting `S4` into `S4a` and `S4b`. **SUPERSEDED by the owner ruling of 2026-08-07 (review 11, finding 1).** The correct fix was to stop treating the retained file store as the rollback source at all: the rollback **data** is the verified receipt rows in `atlas.write_receipts`, and the rollback **code** is the previously merged `S3` build. `S4` is **one build** again, step 9 is deleted, and the `S4a` / `S4b` topology is withdrawn.

**Consistency corrections:** the merge card claimed **31** owner P1 defects while its own table summed to **36** (4+4+5+4+4+4+5+6) — corrected wherever repeated. The §5.5a "decided outcomes" wording now matches the reachable states, resolved by finding 5.

**No counter changed and no authorization was granted by this review.** Campaign 0/5, Phase 5 unauthorized, no schema applied, no deployment.

### ATLAS CONTRACT / SYSTEMS REVIEW 2026-08-07 (tenth required) — exact head `fcafa7513501a104385215365e2678cf0da4076a`: BLOCKING

Review 9's six findings confirmed closed; the exporter architecture again **not reopened**. Six P1 sequencing/topology defects. Five corrected; **one is an OWNER DECISION and is not taken.**

1. **The frozen-drain normalization ran after the gate it unblocks.** §5.5 step 2 demanded zero `in_progress`, and the normalization sat in §5.5a as "step 0" — which executes at step **2a**, behind it. The deadlock it was written to fix survived intact: a rehydrated record fresh at freeze ages past the threshold, stays `in_progress`, and blocks step 2 forever because the fix is unreachable. Corrected: normalization moves **inside the drain**, before its zero assertion, and **repeats until no abandoned record remains** — a record can cross the threshold during the drain. New gate **P19f** seeds a record just under the threshold at freeze.
2. **JSON-only recovery was still allowed when completeness was explicitly unknown.** Recording that the set "may be incomplete" is not a safety mechanism; a field-correct import from a known-incomplete candidate still loses a completed replay or a WRITE-2 `session_id`, just with a note attached. Corrected to **fail closed**: the transfer proceeds only from the live in-memory map or a **previously verified durable capture**. A stale or unverifiable `/tmp` file is **diagnostic evidence only and may not authorize a cutover**. New gate **P19e** proves the cutover aborts when no complete source exists.
3. **The freeze activation mechanism was internally contradictory and unassigned.** Step 1 said the capability ships dormant in `S3`, and a few lines later still said "the freeze ships as its own earlier deploy" — incompatible runbooks, the second recreating the restart window the first closes. `S3`'s own contract also did not require building or proving it, so `S4` depended on code `S3` was not obligated to contain. Corrected: **one path only** — dormant in `S3`, activated by an owner-controlled **runtime switch** requiring no deploy and no restart (an environment variable will not do; a Render env change itself redeploys). **§5.3 now obliges `S3` to ship and prove it on all seven callers**, including that dormant is byte-identical to today. The contradictory sentence is deleted, and no second mechanism is offered as a fallback.
4. **`S4a` could not rely on `/tmp` as its rollback source across its own deploy.** Retaining the *code* does not retain the *data*: step 6 deploys, and this design records that `/tmp/atlas-idempotency.json` may not survive a Render restart — so "the file store is still intact" was unproven after step 6. The reverse path had the same hole, carrying receipts back into `/tmp` and *then* restoring the old build, whose restart could erase the file first. Corrected: the rollback source is the durable set carried at step 2a, restored into the old process **after it is live and before writes reopen**. New gate **P19d** proves both loss cases. *Review 11 finding 4 later named that set concretely — it is `atlas.write_receipts` itself, not a separate "capture" artifact.*
5. **The `S4` proof and deletion gates were not split with the topology.** §5.4 still said "delete, in the same PR" and "nothing survives `S4`", while `S4a` deliberately retains the store, and P16/P17 demanded absence — leaving `S4a` **no truthful passing state**. Corrected at the time by splitting the deletion list, P16, P17, the §7.2 rollback table and the §9 ownership sunset by stage. **SUPERSEDED by the owner ruling of 2026-08-07 (review 11, finding 1):** with the two-build topology withdrawn, `S4` retains nothing, so each of those five surfaces is restored to its single-stage form and the absence gates apply to the one `S4` head.
6. **OWNER DECISION REQUIRED — `S4a` + `S4b` is not executable as one PR.** A pull request has one merge, so removing a store the cutover head retained needs a further merge. Recorded as **decision D6**, recommending five PRs. **D6 was WITHDRAWN by the owner on 2026-08-07** (review 11, finding 1): the question was malformed, because the premise — that the cutover head must retain the legacy store — had already been removed by finding 4 of this same review. The chain stays the owner-approved **four** PRs, `S1 → S2 → S3 → S4`. Dale is not asked to approve a fifth PR to retain code with no surviving consumer.

**No counter changed and no authorization was granted by this review.** Campaign 0/5, Phase 5 unauthorized, no schema applied, no deployment. *D6 was open at the close of this review; the owner withdrew it in review 11 below.*

**One further instance found while applying finding 2, and corrected in the same head.** The **`Exercise_Catalog` sync** had the identical defect in a second place: it is a **permanent** mechanism (the mirror and its sync table outlive the migration) and it also opened `migration_divergences` rows on a content mismatch — a table `S4` drops. Post-`S4` a mismatch now records itself in permanent catalog state (`exercise_catalog_sync.status = 'failed'` with `last_error`), which does not advance currency, so the mirror ages toward `CATALOG_MIRROR_MAX_AGE` and fails Saves closed if unresolved. New gate **P7c0** proves by search that **no permanent mechanism writes to `migration_divergences`** — the class of defect, not just its two instances.
