## OWNER RULING 2026-08-02 — F-SB3: SUBSTITUTION TRUTH (authority defect)

**This ruling is the owner authorization. It governs because it is recorded here.** Dale explicitly authorizes the deterministic coaching behavior required to close this defect, including answering a grounded prescription question ("How much should I lift?") from the engine-owned pending-substitution prescription. **The model gets authority over neither the number nor the mutation.**

**One parent concern:** recommendation, acceptance, canonical mutation, visible claim, and closeout coherence must agree. The narrower **F-SB1-C** and **F-SB2** framings are superseded and folded in; their evidence is retained, and no parallel cards are created.

### The live failure

Workout session `20260801-PM-01`, Flight Recorder `FR-20260802025836-l7hey0gz`, commit `0990c6c`, 02:58:36→03:06:27Z. The save itself was correct: 18 `Log_Cleaned` rows, `sheet_write:"success"`, `closeout_fully_verified:true`, 12 ledger rows sealed, one finalized closeout, no duplicates, no deploy inside the window.

**Atlas visibly claimed a substitution it never performed.** From the recorder's own UI snapshots:

> *"Understood. You're substituting Bench Press with Incline Dumbbell Press."*
> *"Okay, I've noted the substitution. Since you're opting for Incline Dumbbell Press, what weight and reps were you planning for that?"*

The durable ledger disagrees, verified read-only against the live workbook:

| check | result |
|---|---|
| `Session_Plans` events for the session | 6 × `plan_accepted` (SQ01, OHP01, RDL01, **BEN01**, SR01, BC01) + 1 `session_closeout` |
| `item_outcome` events | **0** |
| any `performed_lift_code` = IDB01 | **false** |
| `Session_Plan_Sets` by planned lift | `{SQ01:2, OHP01:2, RDL01:2, BEN01:2, SR01:2, BC01:2}` — BEN01 prescription untouched |
| `Log_Cleaned` | 3 performed IDB01 rows at 90 × 10 RIR 2 |

So Incline DB Press completed as an **inserted** exercise, Bench Press stayed **pending**, the final preview still read *"Still on your plan: Bench Press"*, and the closeout sealed with `closeout_fully_verified:true` over the contradiction.

### Classification: AUTHORITY DEFECT

- **Intended sole authority (winner):** the canonical active-session plan slot, its retained `plan_item_id`, and its durable `Session_Plans` `item_outcome` event.
- **Losing authority (to be removed):** coach prose, pending conversational state, or an independently inserted completed exercise being treated as proof that the plan changed.
- The visible coach may **explain** a canonical mutation. It may never become a second mutation authority. **No permanent reconciliation** between prose and the plan ledger.

### Audit findings (2026-08-02, against `0990c6c`)

**The canonical mutation machinery already exists and is correct.** `applySessionSubstitution` (`src/app/app.js:1645`) reads the original `plan_item_id` off the slot before swapping, retains it on the replacement slot, preserves the accepted set count, checkpoints a bounded future-set revision, and emits `emitPlanItemOutcome({outcome:'substituted', performed_lift_code})`. **It is the intended sole transition. Do not build a third substitution path.** In Dale's session it was simply never called.

**The visible-claim backstop already exists and is already wired.** `coachResponseGrounding.detectUnsupportedMutationClaim` is consumed at `routes/coachOps.js:1785` on `/api/coach/chat`; on a hit it replaces the prose with a grounded statement and drops every proposal. **It has a denylist gap** — measured on `0990c6c`:

| prose | detected? |
|---|---|
| `I've swapped Bench Press for Incline Dumbbell Press.` | **yes** |
| `I updated the plan.` / `The plan has been changed.` | **yes** |
| `I've noted the substitution.` | **NO** — the verb `noted` is absent from `COMPLETED_MUTATION_PATTERNS` |
| `You're substituting Bench Press with Incline Dumbbell Press.` | **NO** — no second-person completed frame exists |

The model happened to choose the two phrasings the denylist misses.

**Extending the denylist is NOT the fix, and the codebase already says so.** `test/coachResponseGrounding.test.js` (~line 367) asserts that a novel completed-write wording is deliberately *not* caught, precisely to prove the denylist is a finite backstop — and the established winning pattern beside it is a **deterministic, model-free answer** (`buildDisputeAnswer`). Substitution needs that same treatment. Patching prose patterns is the same losing strategy as patching routing regexes, which this campaign has now tried twice.

**Why the turn never reached the canonical path.** `looksLikeSessionRequest` claimed *"The bench is taken at the gym so plan give me a substitute workout"* because `sessionQuestion.isWorkoutGenerationRequest` returns **true** on it (the word *"plan"*), and PR #1238 deliberately placed its lift-level guard **after** that classifier. The follow-ups *"No I meant I substitute work out for bench press, like incline dumbbell or something"* and *"Well I was asking for a substitute"* both classify as **null**. Routing may determine which existing operation receives a turn; **routing success is not mutation success**, and no further routing patch closes this card.

### Required lifecycle (A–E)

**A. RECOMMENDATION** — no mutation; Bench Press stays in the plan; the recommendation is a bounded, session-scoped pending proposal. Atlas must **not** say "I changed it", "I noted the substitution", "You're substituting it", "The plan is updated", or equivalent completed-mutation wording.

**B. GROUNDED FOLLOW-UP** — "How much?" / "How many reps?" / "What weight?" are answered **deterministically from the pending proposal's engine-owned prescription**. An ungrounded numbers question never goes to the LLM. Stale, missing, ambiguous, or cross-session proposals **fail closed** with a focused clarification.

**C. ACCEPTANCE** — a clear acceptance binds only to the current session's fresh pending proposal. On acceptance: mutate the canonical slot; retain the original `plan_item_id`; bind IDB01 as performed; emit **exactly one** `item_outcome` (`planned_lift_code=BEN01`, `outcome=substituted`, `performed_lift_code=IDB01`); remove Bench Press from remaining work; make IDB01 satisfy the original slot; clear the proposal; persist the snapshot. **Only after that succeeds** may Atlas emit deterministic confirmation ("Incline Dumbbell Press replaced Bench Press"). The LLM never invents or paraphrases the success claim.

**D. ACCEPTANCE BY LOGGING** — the existing deferred "instead of" binding may remain if it is the canonical path: bind the logged replacement to the original slot **before** accepting it as an unrelated inserted exercise, emitting the same `item_outcome` exactly once. It may never produce *IDB01 inserted + BEN01 pending + a visible success claim*.

**E. FAILURE** — leave the plan unchanged, emit no success wording, preserve the recommendation where safe, tell Dale plainly the plan was not changed, and never silently log the replacement as satisfying the slot.

### Durable ledger rule

**Do not destructively rewrite accepted `Session_Plan_Sets` history** because a substitution occurred. The original BEN01 prescribed sets are valid evidence of what was accepted. The substitution is represented by the retained `plan_item_id`, the canonical `item_outcome`, `performed_lift_code=IDB01`, the bound slot, and the actual IDB01 rows.

### Closeout coherence

**Extend the existing `closeoutVerification` (`index.js`). Do not create a parallel verdict.** `closeout_fully_verified` must be false when the canonical structured state contains a substitution contradiction — at minimum: a structurally accepted substitution with no canonical `item_outcome`; a replacement completed as an inserted exercise while its source slot stays pending under the same accepted proposal; conflicting or duplicate substitution outcomes; a visible success response generated without a successful canonical mutation result; pending accepted replacement state surviving closeout unresolved.

It must **consume canonical mutation state and outcomes** — never infer intent from exercise similarity or freeform prose. **An honestly disclosed unfinished lift must still pass.** The failure condition is the *contradiction*, not incompleteness.

### Authorities to reconcile

`pendingSubstitution` · `pendingReplacement` · `applySessionSubstitution` · `tryApplyImplicitSubstitution` · `checkAndSuggestSubstitute` · `classifyMutationIntent` · `emitPlanItemOutcome` · `emitSetLogged` · `activePlannedSession` · `planSlotStatuses`/`remainingPlannedExercises` · `Session_Plans` persistence · `Session_Plan_Sets` sealing · `closeoutVerification` · visible mutation-claim validation · snapshot hydration/reset.

**Choose one mutation transition.** If `pendingSubstitution` and `pendingReplacement` are competing representations of the same accepted decision, pick one winner and delete the losing production path, or name a narrowly unavoidable bridge with an exact sunset condition. **Two permanent approval systems are not acceptable.**

### Regression proof (must fail pre-fix, pass post-fix)

Dale's exact flow, through the **real browser path** — no new runner: plan includes Bench Press → bench occupied, substitute requested → Atlas recommends Incline DB Press with an engine-owned prescription → Dale asks how much → answered from the pending prescription → Dale accepts or logs IDB01 → substitution applied **exactly once** → IDB01 bound to the original BEN01 `plan_item_id` → BEN01 leaves remaining work → one `substituted` `item_outcome` → the final preview does **not** say *"Still on your plan: Bench Press"* → `closeout_fully_verified` may become true only once state and ledger agree.

**Required negative tests:** recommendation alone neither mutates nor emits completed wording; "How much?" uses the pending engine prescription; a generic "yes" cannot bind to an expired, cross-session, other-exercise, superseded, or post-reset proposal; repeated acceptance is idempotent; repeated logging creates no duplicate `item_outcome`; a mutation failure cannot emit success wording; an unrelated off-plan exercise stays a legitimate insertion; a deliberate skip stays distinct from a substitution; a whole-session alternative stays distinct from a lift-level substitution; reload restores only a valid bounded proposal; closeout refuses Dale's exact contradictory state; closeout still permits an honestly disclosed unfinished lift. **Bite proofs:** removing the `item_outcome` emission, leaving BEN01 pending after accepted IDB01, or returning success prose before mutation must each fail a test.

### PR shape

Prefer **one focused end-to-end PR**. Split only if the diff proves two independently reviewable slices are unavoidable; if split, land the canonical mutation/claim authority first, then the closeout coherence consumer immediately after, name both slices and the final closure condition here, and do no unrelated work between them. Target: one authority reused, the losing claim path deleted, no additional permanent state machine, **net complexity zero or negative**. A small positive change is owner-authorized only for the independent closeout tripwire, offset by deleting the competing claim/mutation path.

### Post-merge protocol

Refresh `main`; verify a clean worktree and no open PR; verify the exact SHA deployed to Render; inspect recent Render logs read-only. **Do not run or simulate an owner workout. Do not delete Dale's test rows without separate explicit authorization. Stage B stays at 0/5.** Stop for Dale's next owner test.

### Secondary — `GET /api/summary/weekly` → 500

Reported at ≈`2026-08-02T03:06:26.608Z`, **post-write**, so it does not threaten the durable save. **Not independently confirmed by the agent yet.** Confirm read-only from Render logs; claim no cause without evidence; fix it in the substitution PR **only** if the identical root cause is mechanically proven, otherwise record one bounded separate finding. It must not delay F-SB3.

### Owner-authorized scope note

Dale's test rows deleted from `Log_Cleaned` up to his last real workout on 2026-07-31 are **his own cleanup**, confirmed by him on 2026-08-02. This explains the lower append range (`A1634` vs `A1677`) and is **not** a data-integrity anomaly.

### F-SB3 — IMPLEMENTED (2026-08-02). One authority, one transition, one verdict.

**Classification: authority defect. One winner selected, the loser removed.**

- **Winner.** The ONE pending proposal (`store.pendingReplacement`, built by `src/app/activeReplacement.js`) decides, and `applySessionSubstitution` is the ONE mutation transition — original `plan_item_id` retained, exactly one canonical `substituted` `item_outcome`. No third substitution path was built.
- **Loser, removed.** The engine lanes' own decision state. `checkAndSuggestSubstitute` no longer parks a cross-turn `pendingSubstitution` and no longer advances the cursor past the unavailable lift; `tryApplyImplicitSubstitution` (now `tryProposeImplicitSubstitution`) no longer mutates on the spot. Both stage the same proposal. The Step 379 skip guard in `currentPlannedExercise` went with them, and `pendingSubstitution` is no longer persisted to or restored from the session snapshot.
- **Bridge, with its sunset.** `pendingSubstitution` survives only as the one-turn "instead of" token (F10S2, permitted by requirement D), armed and consumed inside a single log commit. It folds into the proposal object when `src/app/app.js` is extracted in Phase 5 (H-21).

**Lifecycle A–E, as ruled.** A: the recommendation stages a bounded proposal and mutates nothing — Bench Press stays in the plan and stays current. B: "How much should I lift?" is answered deterministically from the proposal's engine-owned prescription, and says it has no target rather than guessing when the engine resolved none (this closes F-SB2). C: acceptance mutates the canonical slot once, then — and only then — emits a deterministic confirmation. D: logging the replacement binds it to the original slot before it can resolve as an insert. E: a failed mutation says plainly that the plan was not changed.

**The visible claim.** Extending the denylist was rejected as the ruling directs. A substitution turn on the read-only `/api/coach/chat` is now answered deterministically (`coachResponseGrounding.isSubstitutionTurn` / `buildSubstitutionAnswer`, consumed at `routes/coachOps.js`), so the model is never asked and there is no wording to outrun. `COMPLETED_MUTATION_PATTERNS` is unchanged; a test pins that both live phrasings still escape it, which is the point.

**Routing.** One bounded change only: a substitution word plus an unavailability report ("the bench is **taken** … so **plan** give me a **substitute** workout") is a lift-level swap, checked before the generation classifier. The weaker "for|to \<word\>" cue stays below it. This is what lets the owner's own sentence reach the substitution lane; it is not offered as the fix.

**Closeout coherence.** `closeoutVerification` (`index.js`) — the existing single verdict, not a parallel one — now consumes `detectSubstitutionContradictions` (`services/closeoutSummary.js`): a `substituted` outcome binding no performed lift, two slots claiming the same performed lift, or an unresolved substitution proposal over a source slot that recorded neither `completed` nor `substituted`. It reads canonical structured state only. An honestly disclosed unfinished lift still passes — the failure condition is the contradiction, not incompleteness.

**Proof.** `tests/e2e/substitution-truth.spec.js` drives the owner's exact flow through the real browser path and **fails 6/6 on `origin/main`, passes 6/6 after**. `test/substitutionProposalLifecycle.test.js` proves A–E plus every required negative through the real sliced lanes, with four mutation bite proofs: dropping the `item_outcome` emission (4 failures), leaving the source pending after the replacement is logged (2), claiming success without a successful mutation (1), and mutating on recommendation instead of proposing (6). `npm test`, all ten guards, `npm run lint`, and the full 254-test browser suite are green.

**Open, not claimed.** The secondary `GET /api/summary/weekly` → 500 stays **UNCONFIRMED**. Render logs are not reachable from an agent session, so no cause is claimed and nothing was changed for it; code inspection of `routes/reads.js:402` shows no defect independent of a `getRecentRows` read failure. It is not the substitution root cause, so it was correctly excluded from this PR. It needs one read-only log check by a session with Render access.

**Stage B stays at 0/5.** No owner workout was run or simulated, and no owner test rows were touched. The next step is Dale's own Stage B workout.

**Instrument defect — `atlas:review-live` reported a passing session as all-UNKNOWN (trust-critical, tooling).** It correlated **0 Log · 0 Effort · 0 Session_Plans · 0 Session_Plan_Sets** for a session that wrote 12 rows and sealed 24. Cause: the recorder stores request field **names**, never values, so no workout `session_id` is discoverable in the transcript and correlation falls back to dates; `sessionDateSet` (`scripts/flight-review.js`) builds its window **forward only** from the flight session's UTC timestamps. A 17:34 Pacific workout is `2026-08-02` in UTC while its rows are correctly dated `2026-08-01`, so the set was `{2026-08-02, 2026-08-03}` and matched nothing. **Every evening workout breaks it**; the morning session correlated because its UTC and local dates agree. `UNKNOWN` is defined as never-a-pass, so trusting the instrument would have reset a passing streak. This is the second defect found in this tool (the first: text and `--json` runs auto-selecting different sessions). It adjudicates Stage B and should be fixed before workout 2.

**OWNER GATE PASSED 2026-08-01 — STAGE B IS OPEN AT 0/5.** Dale set `SESSION_PLAN_SETS_WRITE_ENABLED=1` on Render and gave the go, which is step (a) of the Phase 4 owner gate script. Step (a) is now asked and answered; it is never asked again.

**Flag state is owner-reported, not agent-verified.** No read-only surface exposes `SESSION_PLAN_SETS_WRITE_ENABLED` — `atlas:status` does not carry it and there is no debug endpoint for it — so this record rests on the owner's statement rather than a measurement, and says so rather than implying a check. **Stage B workout 1 is the verification:** if the flag did not take effect, that workout's evidence shows the W1–W3 dry-run proof instead of planned-set capture, which surfaces the problem after one workout rather than five. Deployed commit at gate time: `9c9389299517de77955091c936b81240157441f9`.

**CORRECTION 2026-08-01 — `ATLAS_TURN_PRECEDENCE` is ON, not off.** This record previously stated the flag stayed off. That was **wrong**. The owner reports he set it to `1` on Render at an unrecorded time and did not know what it does. Stage B workout 1 therefore ran with **both** flags on: `SESSION_PLAN_SETS_WRITE_ENABLED=1` and `ATLAS_TURN_PRECEDENCE=1`. Owner-reported, not agent-verified — no read-only surface exposes either flag.

**What the flag does, and what it did not do here.** `ATLAS_TURN_PRECEDENCE` gates route consumption of the turn-precedence decision in `services/turnPrecedence.js`. Its only current consumer is `POST /api/suggest-substitute`: when a constraint message's referent is a **bare ambiguous pronoun** ("it's broken", "are you broken?"), the decision declines the substitution and the client falls through to the coach. It is an **under-call safety guard** — it can withhold a substitution, never invent one, and it performs no write.

**It is excluded as a cause of F-SB1-C.** Both of the owner's substitution turns were misrouted **client-side** and never called `/api/suggest-substitute` at all, so the flag had nothing to gate. This is read from the Flight Recorder's own call list, not inferred.

**Disposition: owner decision, not an agent call.** Turning a production flag on or off is owner-reserved. It is recorded here as the live posture so no later Stage B evidence is read against the wrong configuration.

**`atlas:status` reads DEGRADED and that is expected here.** Its `LATEST_TEST_FAILED` code points at **LT-012 (2026-07-16)** — the owner live session whose failure drove this entire recovery campaign. It is still the newest owner live test on record because no owner workout has happened since, so the status will stay DEGRADED until Stage B workout 1 supersedes it. It is not a Stage B blocker and must not be read as a new fault.

**Stage B protocol now live.** Workouts are Dale's own, run normally, on production. After workout 1 he takes step (b) minus the Issue #952 part (already recorded closed in `GATE RUN 1 — 2026-07-25`). Workouts 2–5 each take the per-workout verdict script. A miss resets the count to 0/5 and its cause becomes the next card. No agent may produce, simulate, replay, or substitute any of it.

**Stage A is closed at 5/5.** Five consecutive qualifying sessions passed, each from a distinct clean `main` equal to `origin/main`, each model-up with proven live-provider use, each writing exactly 4 `Log_Cleaned` rows and 1 `Effort` row to the declared sandbox with no duplicates and no contamination. **Reaching 5/5 opens nothing.** Stage B is five consecutive OWNER-run workouts and is opened only by the owner via step (a) of the Phase 4 gate script; `SESSION_PLAN_SETS_WRITE_ENABLED` stays `0` until the owner sets it. Phase 5 begins only after Stage B also reaches 5/5 **and** the owner explicitly authorizes it.

**Temporary Stage-A machinery sunset — EXECUTED in the same PR that records 5/5**, exactly as this plan required. Removed: the `ATLAS_GATE_SANDBOX_LIVE` posture and its live-workbook branch in the gate harness, the dedicated Stage-A scenario and scorecard, both operator commands (`atlas:stage-a-canary`, `atlas:stage-a-session`) and their scripts, the run-purpose / run-start / source-facts plumbing, the durable-row verifier endpoint, and the two temporary test suites. **Retained as permanent:** the model-posture authority (`ATLAS_GATE_MODEL_UP`, provider-key deletion, and the refusal to serve a mislabeled posture), the default in-memory Sheets stub that makes a real workbook structurally unreachable from the gate harness, the ledger-sandbox posture, and `config/sandboxSheet.js` — which is consumed by `sheets.js` and `scripts/sim/harness.js` and was never Stage-A machinery.

**Run-duration note (corrected).** Session records 3 and 4 previously attributed a run-duration difference to Sheets read-quota throttling and called the question settled. That claim was unsupported and is withdrawn: the runner's read-retry path never logged, and no captured output contains a quota or rate-limit indication. The durations stand as measurements; the cause is **unknown** and is recorded as an unverified hypothesis. No verdict depended on it.

**Owner ruling 2026-07-31 — authority governance (governance insertion, no phase change).** Two durable records landed together. (1) [`docs/ATLAS_SYSTEM_AUTHORITY.md`](ATLAS_SYSTEM_AUTHORITY.md) is the ownership authority; it replaced `docs/ATLAS_OWNERSHIP_CONNECTIVITY_INVENTORY.md`, which was removed, so exactly one ownership document exists. (2) The **Closed-Loop Delivery Contract** (`CLAUDE.md`) governs delivery: Purpose → Authority → Integration → Proof → Cleanup → Closure, with the no-orphan rule and net-open-loop reporting in the merge card. The architectural ruling is one winner per authority — pick a winner, remove the loser, or name an exact sunset condition; never add permanent reconciliation around competing authorities. **No Phase 5 consolidation is authorized by this insertion.** A concept recorded as `DUPLICATED` or `TRANSITIONAL` is honest bookkeeping, not a work item. The active phase stays Phase 4, and **Stage A: 5/5 — COMPLETE. Stage B: 0/5 — OPEN.** remains unchanged.

**Owner ruling 2026-07-31 — Phase 4 two-stage operational acceptance gate (governance insertion, no phase change).** Recorded below under "OWNER RULING — 2026-07-31 — PHASE 4 TWO-STAGE OPERATIONAL ACCEPTANCE GATE". Phase 4 exits only after Stage A (five consecutive AI-run full sessions, synthetic data, sandbox Sheet) and Stage B (five consecutive owner-run workouts) both pass and the owner explicitly authorizes Phase 5. Stage A precedes Stage B. Current counts: **Stage A: 5/5 — COMPLETE. Stage B: 0/5 — OPEN.** The active phase stays Phase 4. `SESSION_PLAN_SETS_WRITE_ENABLED` stays `0`; Stage A does not require it.

**Owner ruling 2026-07-30, final form 2026-07-31 — bounded backlog ledger (governance insertion, no phase change).** Recorded below under "OWNER RULING — 2026-07-30, FINAL FORM 2026-07-31 — BOUNDED BACKLOG LEDGER" and enforced by Drift Guard 7: four dispositions, and `BACKLOG.md` at fixed capacity. The active phase stays Phase 4.

**Owner instruction 2026-08-03 — the Atlas Contract / Systems Review trigger expands (governance insertion, no phase change).** The instruction governs because it is recorded here. It expands the trigger list of the **existing** review lane recorded in `CLAUDE.md` under "Project decision desk and the Atlas Contract / Systems Review". It creates **no second review system**, no new reviewer account, no new marker, no new required status, and no new CI check. The full rule, the seven review questions, and the four merge-card fields live in `CLAUDE.md`; the `.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md` block is where a PR records them. This insertion is repeated here only to make the trigger list authoritative.

The review is **required** when a PR touches: campaign gates; scorecards and counters; adjudicators; rehearsal and test runners; evidence collectors; identity and correlation machinery; phase or count advancement; or trust-sensitive write, schema, security, promotion, or destructive changes. The earlier triggers stay in force: phase transitions, roadmap changes, product or trust-contract changes, and genuine ambiguity.

The reviewer reads the **exact head** in a clean context. A review of an earlier commit does not cover a later head. The merge card records four fields: required or not required with the trigger that fired; the exact reviewed head as a full commit SHA; the reviewer; and every finding with its disposition.

**Who performs it — OWNER RULING 2026-08-03, governing.** **ChatGPT performs the required Atlas Contract / Systems Review.** The lane's owner is unchanged; the owner instruction broadened the **existing ChatGPT lane's trigger**, not who owns that lane.

An implementation agent may not satisfy its own architecture gate. A clean-context review by the implementation agent is valuable advisory evidence and is recorded under advisory findings, but it never satisfies the required review. The reasoning is recorded so it is not relitigated:

- a clean context is **not an independent authority** — it is still the implementation agent, and it reproduces the same model and system blind spots;
- naming the performer creates **observability, not separation of authority**;
- letting the builder satisfy its own architecture gate creates a **competing authority** and weakens the project-decision-desk contract.

Authority record: current and intended sole authority — **ChatGPT**. Competing authority removed — the implementation-agent clean-context review as a substitute gate satisfier. Bridge — implementation-agent clean-context reviews stay optional and advisory. Sunset — none; this is the permanent authority boundary. Net complexity — decreases, one reviewer authority instead of two.

An earlier draft of this insertion recorded the opposite and was corrected by this ruling before merge.

This insertion selects no work, starts no phase, adds no drift guard, and changes no count. Every streak keeps the value the active `CAMPAIGN STATE:` line records, Phase 5 stays unauthorized, and `SESSION_PLAN_SETS_WRITE_ENABLED` stays untouched. This paragraph deliberately states **no streak numerals**: `parseCanonicalRehearsalCount` (`tests/e2e/gate/rehearsal-run-purpose.js`) reads the canonical rehearsal count out of this document, and a second copy of that count written in the marker's own format would be one careless edit away from either a false red (two current markers) or a false green (a stale copy read as current). One count lives in one place.

**Owner instruction 2026-08-05 — the implementation role is surface-neutral and model-neutral (governance insertion, no phase change).** The instruction governs because it is recorded here. Atlas work is implemented by the **approved active implementation agent**: a role, not a product name. Whichever agent Dale approves holds it, on whichever surface it runs — Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, or another owner-approved implementation surface — and whichever model that surface runs. The canonical definition lives in `CLAUDE.md`; every other governance surface points at it. Active wording that granted the role to a named tool alone is removed, not kept beside the general contract. `CLAUDE.md` keeps its historical filename and stays the one canonical brief; `AGENTS.md` is the universal entrypoint; `CODEX.md` and any surface pointer file carry no implementation authority. Historical records and retained filenames — `claude/*` branches, `.claude/rules/*`, dated evidence — still name the agent that did the work, and rewriting them would falsify the record.

The unchanged roles are restated because they are the reason the role can move at all: Dale owns product direction, owner-only evidence, production-write authorization, schemas, destructive changes, security and credentials, runtime/provider/model selection, and final phase progression. ChatGPT is Atlas's architectural owner and decision desk, and ChatGPT performs the required Atlas Contract / Systems Review whenever a trigger fires. An independent builder-model review is advisory and never substitutes for a required deterministic gate or for that review. The selected surface and model change no branch rule, no one-concern discipline, no testing requirement, no trust contract, no owner-reserved stop, no exact-head review, and no merge authority.

Two additions carry it. (1) The existing Atlas Merge Card gains four required attribution fields — **Builder surface**, **Primary builder model**, **Supporting / explore models**, **Architecture / dispatch authority** — and the existing merge-card completeness check fails a PR that leaves one absent, blank, or a template placeholder. Attribution is declared evidence, not cryptographic proof; it records who acted and grants no authority. The merge card is the sole attribution authority: no commit trailer, model registry, label taxonomy, or tracking database is created. (2) `docs/BUILDER_PORTABILITY.md` gains the read-only fresh-agent cold-start acceptance trial and its ten required report items. **No portability PASS is claimed here.** Structural readiness is not proof; a PASS requires a real fresh agent on a new surface to perform the trial after this insertion merges.

This insertion selects no work, starts no phase, adds no drift guard, creates no roadmap, workflow, agent registry, model database, second merge card, or second review system, and changes no count. Phase 5 stays unauthorized and `SESSION_PLAN_SETS_WRITE_ENABLED` stays untouched. It states no streak numerals, for the reason given in the paragraph above.

**Owner clarification 2026-08-05 — the portability trial is deferred, not a prerequisite (sequencing correction, no phase change).** The clarification governs because it is recorded here. The insertion above delivered surface-neutral implementation authority and merge-card attribution. It did **not** authorize a surface switch, and it did not make the fresh-agent cold-start acceptance trial the next step. PR #1268 was completed and merged on the opposite reading, which is a governance sequencing defect. This paragraph is the correction.

Four facts hold until Dale records otherwise here:

1. **Claude remains the approved active implementation agent**, until at least **2026-08-17**. The role is surface-neutral, and Dale has not elected to move it. Surface-neutral means the role *can* move on an owner instruction; it does not mean it *has* moved.
2. **Cursor portability is structurally ready, and it is not live-proven.** The repository documents the trial, the launcher, the ten report items, and the six bounds. No agent has performed it. No portability PASS exists anywhere in this repository, and none may be claimed, recorded, or implied until a real fresh agent on a new surface actually runs the trial and reports all ten items correctly.
3. **The trial is a deferred portability acceptance proof.** It runs when Dale elects to switch surfaces, or when Dale explicitly requests it. It is not scheduled, and no card depends on it.
4. **The absence of a Cursor trial blocks nothing.** It does not block Claude implementing, testing, opening PRs, or merging under standing authority, and it does not block F-SB4B, qualifying Session 1, or any other campaign work. Campaign work proceeds on its own eligibility. Any statement that F-SB4B or qualifying Session 1 waits for the trial is void, wherever it appears — including in the merged body of PR #1268 and in any session report.

This clarification weakens nothing. Every owner-reserved stop, safety rule, write gate, exact-head Atlas Contract / Systems Review trigger, merge-card attribution rule, and repository-state handoff requirement stays exactly as recorded. It grants no permission to perform or simulate the trial, and simulating it would be a fabricated PASS.

Like the insertion above, this clarification selects no work, starts no phase, adds no drift guard, changes no count, and states no streak numerals. Phase 5 stays unauthorized and `SESSION_PLAN_SETS_WRITE_ENABLED` stays untouched.

**#1165 slice 2 foundation (supersedes the older inline `REMAINING ON #1165` / `BLOCKED` notes in the historical campaign ledger below).** Slice 2 is complete: the client now creates a bounded initiation nonce before every preview, associates the server's canonical `turn_id` and pairing capability with that initiation, explicitly retires superseded initiations, and retains only the newest-initiated preview independent of response completion order. Duplicate transport attempts for one initiation reuse its immutable pairing, so a late original preview cannot replace the capability already returned by its retry; a different payload under that nonce fails closed without consuming the pairing. If a payload exceeds the bounded identity-work limit, a later same-initiation request is never assumed equal merely because neither identity could be computed; it fails closed while the underlying preview remains unchanged. Coach-response headers are committed only after that request wins the visible-response/timeout race, so a late `/api/coach/ask`, `/api/coach/chat`, or `/api/coach/message` completion cannot overwrite the selected turn. The real composer rechecks its submit generation after every asynchronous routing fallthrough, and the two substitute helpers recheck that authority immediately after their network await and before changing plan/session truth, so an older non-loggable submit cannot resume after a newer preview to mutate the plan, stage a pending substitution, or mint a response that retires that preview. The additive `correlation` envelope is carried through manual/slash, screenshot, effort-only, modality, bodyweight, and closeout/seal-retry approvals; a blank-session effort-only preview explicitly adopts the server-resolved next session before approval, without permitting an ordinary cross-session claim. Missing, malformed, forged, stale, superseded, payload-mismatched, and cross-session claims fail closed in the evidence seam while the underlying write remains unchanged. The documented seal retry still mints a fresh `write_id` and may remove only the already-written `effort_row`. Pairing capabilities and payload fingerprints remain excluded from response bodies, logs, traces, and artifacts. Phase 4 remains **NOT YET**; no owner gate is authorized, `SESSION_PLAN_SETS_WRITE_ENABLED` remains `0`, and Phase 5 has not begun.

Structured chat proposals use the same initiation authority: after prose rendering and immediately before each edit, plan edit, note, constraint, or prescription-placeholder side effect, the client rechecks that the accepted response ticket is still the single global owner of the shared UI. Beginning a newer preview or response synchronously revokes that authority across sessions, so stale structured output cannot mutate or clear the retained preview or overwrite S2's composer placeholder after the user has moved from S1 to S2. A response that cannot mint a bounded ticket (including a malformed or oversized session id) has no structured-effect authority; missing authority fails closed instead of falling back to permissive behavior. Every set-log path, including a held-clarification affirmation, binds its rows to the current logging session and synchronizes the selected `/api/coach/message` response before an immediate closeout, so the resulting turn replaces any older turn in that session and is the turn carried through closeout preview and approval. The whole set-response pipeline is bounded, including the recommendation read before `/api/coach/message`; rejected, timed-out, disconnected, stalled-pre-message, and early-fallback paths settle that wait. The watchdog explicitly cancels a still-running ticket, so its asynchronous set pipeline cannot resume after a late dependency resolves. An ordinary correlation failure that has already resolved may still finish the existing deterministic fallback while remaining uncorrelated. A valid response selected before closeout may finish its already-selected prose, but it rechecks current ownership before any later prescription, handoff, or placeholder effect. A failed selected response clears the stale turn and closeout proceeds without a correlation claim, leaving the underlying preview and write behavior unchanged.

**Current #1165 status — slice 3 complete in this change.** `npm run atlas:turn-write-artifact -- <logfile> [--json]` builds the bounded, read-only review artifact joining `[interaction-trace]` and `[turn-write-proof]` only on the existing canonical `turn_id`. The consumer re-whitelists the untrusted log stream, accepts only the trace producer's minted timestamp/sequence/random `turn_id` shape, and emits `intent_type` / `source` only from the fixed production trace vocabularies. It retains the documented bounded seal retry as a distinct write attempt, requires preview-established payload binding plus positive write or explicit no-write evidence before calling a join reviewable, and never emits pairing capabilities, payload fingerprints, row/prompt/prose fields, Sheet ids, or arbitrary nested fields. Every live main-write route requires a coherent `sheet_write:'success'` state before positive Sheet/count signals can substantiate a write. For live `/api/log-workout` attempts, each positive Log/Effort row count must be paired with append-range evidence bound to the correct tab, exact column shape, and matching row span; the response's `effortWritten` formatting/bookkeeping boolean never substantiates an append by itself. Conflicting non-null session ids on one canonical turn fail reviewability closed, and any rejected marker record makes the overall artifact partial even when the accepted join is otherwise complete. Closeout evidence carries its validated `plan_version` row discriminator; a written or idempotently skipped closeout with that discriminator absent or withheld is unidentified and not reviewable. Seal states are explicit and non-interchangeable: a fresh stamp is `sealed`, an idempotent replay is `already_sealed`, and `sheet_written:true` with `sealed_ok:false` is `seal_proof_mismatch` with `successfully_sealed:false`. Projection failures now add only a fixed-name `withheld_evidence` marker, never the rejected value, so the artifact distinguishes validation-withheld evidence from genuinely absent evidence without weakening projection validation. Empty and partial joins exit non-zero and cannot be read as a false green. Evidence: `test/turnWriteArtifact.test.js`, `test/turnWriteArtifactIntegration.test.js` (actual minted trace id + trace + registry/pairing/write-proof emitters), and the lanes-on `test/turnWriteProofCloseoutIntegration.test.js` (genuine stamp, replay, and conflicting seal). Phase 4 remains **NOT YET**; no owner gate is authorized, `SESSION_PLAN_SETS_WRITE_ENABLED` remains `0`, and Phase 5 has not begun.

`CAMPAIGN STATE: Phase 4 — The canonical proof. FIRST CONCERN ✅ LANDED 2026-07-23 (substitution lane): one authoritative current-message decision (`services/turnPrecedence.js`) now owns whether a turn requests a substitution, retiring the substitution lane's route-local `intent || isConstraintMessage` interpretation that let a malfunction complaint ("Are you broken?" → `/\bbroken\b/`) invoke substitution and contradict the active plan (Phase-3 divergence D5/D6; FR-20260723120852-hw56ws9y turn 4). FIRST-CONCERN HARDENING ✅ LANDED 2026-07-23 (ambiguous pronoun-only malfunction): the substitution decision now requires AUTHORITATIVE REFERENT EVIDENCE — an ambiguous pronoun-only malfunction ("it's broken", "is it broken?", "it is broken") no longer authorizes a swap, because its only referent is a bare pronoun that could mean Atlas OR the equipment (particularly right after an incorrect answer). The prior disambiguator vetoed only a SECOND-PERSON address ("are YOU broken?") and let the impersonal "it" through to the constraint path, producing a plan-changing swap on a guess. `decideTurnPrecedence` now treats a constraint as a substitution ONLY when a referent is authoritative — the parser recognizes a named lift/equipment (`messageNamesALift` — "the bench is broken", "the squat rack is broken") OR the subject is a concrete noun matching none of the ambiguous-pronoun tokens ("the cable machine is not working", "the rack is not working") — otherwise it fails closed to the aside lane; a genuine explicit `intent:'substitute'` still substitutes. This also CORRECTS the retired "introduces no new phrase regex" claim: the module owns ONE narrow, accurately-documented disambiguator (`AMBIGUOUS_PRONOUN_REFERENT_RE`), gated on the parser's authoritative named-lift evidence. Under-call, never fabricate — superseded when `discussion_referent` becomes a canonical CoachTurnPacket field (D10 punch list). Evidence: `test/turnPrecedence.test.js` (ambiguous-pronoun fail-closed regression + named-referent still-substitutes split) and `test/api-smoke.test.js` (real `/api/suggest-substitute` regressions — flag ON ⇒ no substitution / `turn_precedence.lane:'aside'`; flag OFF ⇒ byte-identical prior substitution and no `turn_precedence` envelope; zero Sheet writes both ways). H-08A BOUNDARY FIX ✅ LANDED 2026-07-24 (an un-coded client session no longer sinks to null): the live client active-session model carries `liftCode` as a STRING and uses `''` for "no lift code" (`createActiveSession`→`toEntry` normalizes an absent code to `''`), but the canonical WorkoutSession contract requires `lift_code` to be `null` or a NON-EMPTY string. `fromActiveSession` passed `''` straight through, so a real session with even one un-coded exercise (the common case — most planned lifts carry no code) failed `validateWorkoutSession` and `buildCanonicalSessionSnapshot` returned null — meaning H-08A's `packet.session` would almost never populate in production. `services/workoutSession.js::fromActiveSession` now bridges the two representations faithfully (`_canonicalLiftCode`: trim a STRING, coerce empty/whitespace → null; prefer `liftCode` then `lift_code`), so an un-coded session VALIDATES and the H-08A shadow packet.session populates for real sessions. The empty→null coercion is deliberately STRING-ONLY — a present non-string code (a number/object) is a malformed snapshot left UNCHANGED so `validateWorkoutSession` still rejects it (fail closed; Codex #1146 P2). Pure contract-adapter fix; no flag, no route change, no behavior change beyond the boundary now validating what it should. Old clients that omit `active_session` fall back to the `plan_state` path unchanged. It merged with TWO ACCEPTED P1 FINDINGS UNFIXED, recorded as #1173 (a merged PR thread is not where work gets selected from). #1173 ITEM 1 ✅ LANDED 2026-07-25 (preview-established binding): the pairing is now established at the PREVIEW and keyed on a server-minted `pair:` token returned on `x-atlas-turn-pairing`, retiring #1172's first-write-wins — which let the CLIENT choose which write a turn bound to and, worse, LOCKED THE LEGITIMATE WRITE OUT with `write_mismatch` when a turn id landed on the wrong same-session write, making a client bug cost LOST evidence rather than wrong evidence. The prerequisite #1173 flagged was CHECKED, NOT ASSUMED, and does NOT hold: `write_id` is shared between preview and approve on ONE of five write paths (`/api/log-workout`, app.js:6960→7506), is withheld from the dry-run by design on both `/api/complete-workout` paths (app.js:7113), is minted only after the preview on modality and bodyweight (app.js:6133, 7790), and is DELIBERATELY RE-MINTED mid-flow on the documented seal retry even where it is shared (app.js:7563-7568) — so a `write_id` key would have been correct on one route of five and would have re-created the very lockout it was meant to remove. The previewed `write_id` is therefore recorded as CORROBORATION (`previewed_write_id_match`) and never as a gate. A TOKEN ALONE IS ONLY A TURN-LEVEL BINDING, and the first cut of this card claimed a write-level one — Codex refuted that on review (#1174 r3649520130) and was right: a valid token on a DIFFERENT same-session payload was accepted, so the record proved only "this turn previewed something, and this write presented its token". Corrected in the same PR by a SERVER-COMPUTED payload identity: the preview fingerprints the write identity the server itself received (`session_id` + `date` + `log_rows`) and the live payload must reproduce it, depending on nothing the client mints, so it holds on every route rather than one of five; The fingerprint is DEFAULT-DENY over the whole payload; only `test_mode`, `write_id` and `correlation` are outside it, each being a field that provably differs between a preview and its own approve (app.js:7507, 7566). `effort_row` is INSIDE the fingerprint — it is write-affecting (index.js:2930) — and the seal retry's effort REMOVAL is one explicit permitted transition, legal only after this pairing has already accepted an exact-identity write; a CHANGED or newly-ADDED effort row is refused. (An earlier draft of this entry said `effort_row` was excluded outright, describing a superseded round of this same PR; corrected here because the plan is the sole work-selection authority and slice 2 must not implement the obsolete exclusion.) Codex also caught (P2) that superseding the pairing per preview was last-COMPLETION-wins while the client keeps the newest by INITIATION order (`previewRequestSeq`/`submitSeq`, app.js:6416/6881), so an older preview finishing last discarded the token the client kept — replaced by a bounded set of the turn's outstanding pairings. Honest ceiling, stated in the module: this proves "this turn previewed a write of exactly this identity, and this write presented that preview's token"; where no identity is computable the pairing degrades to turn-level and reports `payload_bound:false` rather than an assumed match. Neither form is cryptographic authorization, which no client round-trip can deliver. Evidence: `test/turnCorrelation.test.js` (+`unpaired`/`pairing_mismatch`/`pairing_exhausted`/`payload_mismatch`, overlapping-preview ordering, bounded outstanding pairings, seal-retry-not-locked-out, key-order-insensitive identity, unbound-degrades-honestly, token and fingerprint never logged, `write_mismatch` removal pinned), `test/turnCorrelationIntegration.test.js` (real-route preview→token→approve; unpaired, forged-token, and valid-token-on-a-different-workout live writes correlate NOTHING and still write their rows; seal-retry shape still joins; CORS exposure). #1173 ITEM 2 ✅ LANDED 2026-07-25 (closeout proof projections): the all-rows-duplicate branch correlates when `ledger_seal` or `session_plans_closeout` is present — because with the Session Plan lanes enabled it genuinely appends the closeout event and stamps the seal — but NEITHER envelope was in `PROOF_KEYS` and both are nested objects the scalar-only filter drops, so the record emitted for that branch reported zero rows and NO seal evidence at all: a trigger added without its evidence, unable to prove the very write it was added to capture. `PROOF_PROJECTIONS` now flattens each envelope to `<envelope>_<field>` under a closed per-envelope whitelist, namespaced so a projection can never collide with a top-level proof key, with every value still passing the same scalar-only filter (the whitelist decides WHICH fields, the filter decides WHAT SHAPE). `session_plans_closeout.plan_version` IS projected because it is hashed into `sessionPlanEvents.idempotencyKey` (services/sessionPlanEvents.js:74) and is therefore the closeout event's ROW DISCRIMINATOR — without it a session with more than one accepted plan version leaves the record unable to say WHICH `session_closeout` row was written, and the slice-3 artifact join could not substantiate its own turn→closeout claim; it was excluded in the first draft as "a plan token rather than write proof" and Codex corrected that (being the discriminator is what makes it write proof). It is CLIENT-SUPPLIED — minted client-side as `pv_`+UUID (src/app/planAcceptance.js `mintId`) and accepted by the server as anything matching `/^pv_.+/` straight off the request body (index.js:2842), so an intermediate claim of mine that it was "server-generated" was simply FALSE — and projecting it unvalidated made the record a pass-through for arbitrary client text up to the body limit; it is now gated on the canonical `pv_`+UUID shape, and a generic `MAX_PROJECTED_STRING_LENGTH` bounds EVERY projected string as a structural backstop, since each enumeration proving a field fixed-vocabulary is a property of code that can change. Deliberately NOT projected: the seal's `conflicting_write_ids` (array) and `diagnostics` (object), and — the load-bearing omissions — the seal's `error` and the closeout's `reason`, both of which carry an ARBITRARY exception message (index.js wraps a seal throw as `{ reason:'seal_error', error: String(error.message) }`; `sessionPlanCapture._capture` sets `reason: e.message`) that could carry a Sheet id or other internals into a telemetry line; the seal's own `reason` IS projected because every value it takes in `sessionPlanSetsStore` is a fixed vocabulary literal, and the closeout's fixed vocabulary lives on `status`. Evidence: `test/turnCorrelation.test.js` (seal and closeout projections incl. an honestly-projected FAILED seal; closed-projection proof that diagnostics/ids/error text never survive; nested-value-on-a-whitelisted-field still dropped; non-object envelope projects nothing and never throws; namespacing proven collision-free against `PROOF_KEYS`). Item 2's review ALSO corrected two P2s rather than deferring them: (a) the `buildWriteProofRecord` contract still declared that every proof field is copied verbatim and never renamed, which the projection loop contradicts — now split into the two mechanisms it actually has (top-level = verbatim under `PROOF_KEYS`; envelopes = deliberately renamed projections that ADD namespaced keys and never reshape a W1–W3 field), because a stale invariant is worse than none; (b) the headline unit fixture combined a successful stamp's `sheet_written:true`/`sealed:5` with the all-sealed branch's `reason:'all_sealed'` — a state `sealCloseout` CANNOT return — so every fixture is now an attainable shape copied from `sealCloseout`/`_envelope`, including the distinguishable idempotent all-sealed replay. Two further honesty cases: an idempotent replay that seals nothing new stays DISTINGUISHABLE from a fresh stamp in the record (`sealed_ok:true` + `sheet_written:false` + `sealed:0` + a positive `already_sealed`), so an artifact can never read a replay as a new sealed write; and a CONFLICTING seal fails closed with `sealed_ok:false`/`closeout_fully_verified:false` carried into the record while the foreign closeout's write id — an array the projection does not whitelist — never reaches it. #1173 IS NOW COMPLETE (all three items). SLICE-3 CONSUMER NOTE: a projected field that fails its validator is currently DROPPED, so a consumer cannot distinguish "present but withheld as malformed" from "absent"; if the artifact join needs that distinction, slice 3 is where an explicit withheld marker belongs (deliberately not added inside item 2, which would have changed the reviewed head). SECOND REVIEW ROUND on the same PR produced two more real findings, both fixed or recorded rather than waved off: (a) P1 — the payload fingerprint was a default-ALLOW list (`session_id`+`date`+`log_rows`), but `/api/log-workout` also APPENDS `effort_row` (index.js:2930) and drives the closeout capture and ledger seal from `closeout_context` (index.js:3172, 3320), so a live request could change either and still be reported `payload_bound:true`; the fingerprint is now DEFAULT-DENY (the whole payload minus `test_mode`/`write_id`/`correlation`, each of which provably differs between a preview and its own approve), with the seal retry's effort REMOVAL as ONE explicit permitted transition that still refuses a CHANGED or newly-ADDED effort row. (b) P2 — NOT CLOSED, recorded as a **REQUIREMENT ON SLICE 2**: pairing eviction is still by COMPLETION order, so a finite set cannot GUARANTEE it keeps the pairing the client kept — if more than `MAX_OUTSTANDING_PAIRINGS` previews overlap AND the newest-initiated completes first, its token is evicted first while the client discarded every other response by initiation sequence. Raising the cap (now 8) shrinks the window but cannot remove it; only INITIATION IDENTITY or explicit retirement FROM THE CLIENT can, and inventing that field inside item 1 would design slice 2's contract from the wrong end. **Slice 2 must therefore carry a preview initiation nonce (or explicit retirement) and retire this gap.** The failure mode is bounded and one-directional — a LOST correlation, never a wrong one, and the write proceeds untouched — so no record can overclaim because of it. THIRD REVIEW ROUND produced two more real P1s, both fixed: (a) the effort-removal transition was permitted unconditionally, so the VERY FIRST live request could silently omit the previewed Effort append and still be recorded `payload_bound:true` having never performed the previewed write — it is now gated on the pairing having already accepted an EXACT-identity write (the real retry cannot precede one: app.js deletes `effort_row` only after a committed write returned `closeout_fully_verified:false`, app.js:7563-7567), and the relaxed match is recorded as `effort_transition` so a reviewer can tell an exact binding from a relaxed one; (b) only the TOP-LEVEL session was checked, but `normalizeLogRowObject` resolves each row as `row.session_id || row.sessionId || topLevelSessionId` (index.js:449) — a row-level id WINS — and an array `effort_row` is written verbatim (index.js:541-545), so a request could write rows under session B while the record named session A, i.e. cross-session contamination inside the evidence itself; every EXPLICIT row-level and effort-row session identity must now equal the bound session or the correlation is refused, at preview as well as on the live write. FOURTH REVIEW ROUND found the third path in that same gate (also P1, fixed): `log_rows` also accepts the POSITIONAL Log_Cleaned array form, where `logRowArrayToObject` sets `session_id: row[1]` with NO top-level fallback (index.js:512), so a positional row's session is ALWAYS explicit — including an empty one, which is written as an empty session_id rather than inherited — and skipping non-object rows left it wholly unchecked; the check also failed OPEN on a PRESENT non-string session, because `normalizeLogRowObject` accepts any TRUTHY value while the gate tested for a string. Both shapes are now compared by rendered value (fail-closed), with object-row FALSY values correctly treated as inheriting the already-checked top level and whitespace correctly treated as a real mismatch (truthy, so it wins the `||`). FIFTH REVIEW ROUND found the fourth shape (also P1, fixed): the OBJECT-form `effort_row` has semantics OPPOSITE to an object log row — `normalizeEffortRow` selects aliases by PROPERTY PRESENCE via `effortRowFieldAliases` (`hasOwnProperty`, index.js:548-555), not truthiness — so a FALSY `session_id` is written VERBATIM to the Effort session column rather than inheriting, and reusing the log-row truthiness rule skipped exactly those values; the comparison now uses presence and the SAME alias normalization selects (first present, in the contract's own order, sourced from `config/columns` so a new alias is covered automatically). ENUMERATION AUDITED AND CLOSED for session identity: object log rows, positional log rows, array `effort_row`, object `effort_row` — and `closeout_context` verified as NOT a fifth path, because `recordCloseoutEvent` (index.js:2847) and `sealCloseout` both take the TOP-LEVEL `session_id` explicitly while the context supplies only `plan_version` and `items`. SIXTH AND SEVENTH ROUNDS closed two more (both fixed): a PREVIEW record reported `payload_bound:true` merely because an identity was computable, before any live payload existed to compare — it is now true only after a live comparison actually matched, so a preview-only flow can never publish the write-level claim; and the session gate TRIMMED before comparing, making it MORE PERMISSIVE THAN THE WRITE PATH, which writes row sessions verbatim (`normalizeLogRowObject` keeps the truthy value; positional Log and Effort rows pass straight through) and does not trim the top-level `session_id` either (index.js:2865) — so `'S1 '` matched a bound `'S1'` while the row was appended under `'S1 '`. Padded session values are now REFUSED rather than normalized, at row level and top level alike, because equivalence-by-trim is not a property the write path has. Row-level `date` is deliberately NOT gated: it cannot contaminate the session the record names, and the payload fingerprint already covers it for write-identity purposes. ## OWNER INSERTION 2026-08-02 — F-SB4: FIVE-SESSION OWNER-PATTERN AI REHEARSAL

**This insertion is the owner authorization. It governs because it is recorded here, not because it was said in a session.** Dale requires one more five-session AI-run rehearsal before he attempts the next Stage B owner workout.

**What this insertion does not do.** It does not reopen Stage A. It does not alter the completed Stage A 5/5 record. It is not Stage B evidence. It cannot advance Stage B. **Stage A: 5/5 — COMPLETE. Stage B: 0/5 — OPEN. Phase 5 stays unauthorized.** `SESSION_PLAN_SETS_WRITE_ENABLED` stays `0`. No Sheet schema changes. No Render flag changes. No production workbook, owner data, or owner workout. **No simulation may be described as owner evidence.**

**Rehearsal streak field.** Record the count in `CAMPAIGN STATE` as `Rehearsal (F-SB4): <k>/5.` beside the existing stage streaks. It is a third, separate counter.

### Purpose

Run five consecutive model-up synthetic full sessions built around Dale's actual conversational style, the exact phrasings he used, the workflows where Atlas has repeatedly become confused, and the trust seams the first Stage B attempts exposed.

This is not five copies of the Stage A happy path. Each session exercises different owner-like friction and still completes a full workout through: real browser → real built client → real local Express → current production parser/router/session/coach/validator → the exact declared sandbox workbook → preview → real browser approval → durable sandbox write → closeout → evidence review.

Use fresh synthetic identities only.

### Card shape

**F-SB4 — Owner-pattern AI rehearsal.** One closed chain with three ordered slices. It is not another campaign, roadmap, backlog, or governance system.

- **F-SB4A — Truthful adjudicator.** Fix the `atlas:review-live` correlation defect first.
- **F-SB4B — Five consecutive owner-pattern sessions.**
- **F-SB4C — Record 5/5 and remove the temporary execution machinery.**

**Do not create a second runner.** Reuse the existing Playwright runner, the existing gate server, the existing browser/client/backend paths, the existing trace and turn-write-proof formats, and the existing sandbox declaration.

The former Stage A commands and the live-sandbox posture were deliberately sunset (recorded above). **Do not permanently resurrect them.** If real durable sandbox proof cannot be achieved with current machinery, restore only the smallest necessary temporary pieces from the previously proven Stage A implementation in repository history. **Every restored temporary piece is removed in F-SB4C after 5/5.**

**Expected net permanent complexity:** the review-tool defect fixed; valuable deterministic regression tests retained; temporary operator and live-sandbox machinery removed; no new production authority; no permanent second test framework.

### F-SB4A — fix the adjudicator first

Resolve the known `atlas:review-live` correlation defect before Session 1, unless current-state verification proves it already fixed. The defect is recorded above: an evening Pacific workout can be dated the next day in UTC; the rows keep the correct local date; `sessionDateSet` builds only forward from UTC timestamps; the tool can therefore correlate zero rows and report all `UNKNOWN`; and text and `--json` invocations have selected different automatic sessions.

**This tool adjudicates the rehearsal. It cannot stay unable to identify an evening session.** Classification: **local tooling defect**. Fix it in one focused PR before building or running the five sessions.

Required behavior:

1. An explicitly supplied Flight Recorder or session identity always wins.
2. Text and JSON output select the same session.
3. Correlation handles both local dates that can intersect the UTC evidence window.
4. The tool never silently correlates another session.
5. `UNKNOWN` remains never-a-pass.
6. Ambiguous correlation fails closed and reports why.
7. Tests cover a Pacific evening workout whose UTC date is the following day.
8. A mutation proof shows the old forward-only date logic fails.

Do not expose request values or private workout content to repair correlation. After merging F-SB4A, refresh clean `main` before constructing the rehearsal.

### Qualifying execution

All five sessions are: model-up; live-provider proven positively; run from clean `main` equal to `origin/main`; run against the exact declared sandbox; isolated by fresh athlete, session, run, turn, preview, pairing, and write identities; free from an inherited ambient `GOOGLE_SHEETS_ID`; executed through the real browser; approved through the visible review-card Save control; durably verified from sandbox rows; reviewed by the corrected adjudicator; bounded and privacy-safe.

Each scenario declares its expected exercises, sets, events, rows, and closeout result **before** execution. The scorecard compares actual evidence against that declared expectation. A generic "some rows appeared" standard does not qualify.

Provider use and deterministic grounding are proven separately. Provider proof shows a real Gemini response on a turn that legitimately uses the model. Engine-owned numbers, state, mutations, remaining work, and write claims must not rest on model provenance.

### The five scenarios

**Session 1 — Dale's exact substitution flow.** A six-exercise accepted plan: Back Squat, Overhead Press, Romanian Deadlift, Bench Press, Seated Row, Bicep Curl. Progress until Bench Press is current, then use his exact wording: *"The bench is taken at the gym so plan give me a substitute workout"*. Require: it is a lift-level substitution request, not whole-session generation; Atlas recommends a deterministic replacement and prescription; the recommendation alone mutates nothing; Bench Press stays current until acceptance; no completed-mutation wording appears; exactly one bounded `pendingReplacement` proposal exists. Then ask *"Nice! How much should I lift?"* and require the answer to come from the pending proposal's engine-owned prescription, not an earlier completed lift, with no model-invented lift, weight, reps, sets, or RIR. Log the replacement in his normal shorthand and require: the replacement binds to the original Bench Press `plan_item_id`; exactly one `substituted` `item_outcome`; `performed_lift_code` identifies the replacement; Bench Press leaves remaining work; the replacement is not merely an inserted exercise; the final preview does not say Bench Press remains; `closeout_fully_verified` is true only after canonical state and ledger agree. Finish, preview, approve, write, verify, and close the full session.

**Session 2 — conversational correction.** Exercise how Dale corrects Atlas. Use *"No I meant a substitute workout for bench press, like incline dumbbell or something"* and *"Well I was asking for a substitute"* naturally inside a full session. Require: a conversational "No" never becomes a skip; the request never becomes whole-session generation; Atlas does not change subjects; Atlas does not raise an unrelated historical Bench Press concern; one current bounded proposal is kept; no claim that the substitution was noted before acceptance. Ask a short follow-up such as *"How much?"* and require a deterministic proposal-grounded answer. Accept with natural shorthand such as *"Yes use that"* and require: generic acceptance binds only to the fresh current-session proposal; exactly one mutation; a repeated "yes" does not duplicate the `item_outcome`; stale, superseded, or cross-session proposal state cannot participate. Complete the workout and verify preview, write, closeout, and exact durable state.

**Session 3 — replacement second in a batch.** Reproduce the Codex P1 edge at full-session level. Stage a legitimate replacement proposal, then submit a multi-exercise log where another valid exercise appears first, the proposed replacement appears second, and optionally one genuinely unrelated off-plan exercise appears separately. Require: the binder examines every logged exercise, not only the first; the proposed replacement binds to the source planned slot; the unrelated off-plan exercise stays a legitimate insertion; Atlas does not infer substitutions from similarity; exactly one `substituted` `item_outcome`; no duplicate outcome when the batch or the acceptance repeats; the source lift does not stay pending; remaining-work and next-up state are correct. Ask *"What do I have left?"* and require the answer to reflect canonical plan state, not chat memory or model inference. Complete preview, approval, durable write, closeout, and review.

**Session 4 — save, closeout, and honest incompleteness.** Run a full workout in Dale's normal style but deliberately leave one planned lift unfinished, or end early with a natural statement such as *"I'm done for today"* or *"I'm wiped today"*. Require: Atlas reports remaining work honestly; it does not fabricate completion; an honestly unfinished lift is not treated as a substitution contradiction; closeout may verify when the durable state honestly represents what happened. Supply no Apple Watch or effort data, and require: no `Effort` row is expected; the absence of `Effort` does not fail the workout write; the UI does not stay on "Saving…"; the visible button reaches the correct completed state; one approved save writes exactly once; repeated approval creates zero duplicate rows; session state tears down cleanly; the next session cannot inherit this session's proposal, logs, or pending state. After the write, call `GET /api/summary/weekly` and require HTTP 200 with a valid bounded response. **If the previously observed 500 reproduces:** preserve the exact exception and evidence, classify it, fix it in its own focused PR, reset the rehearsal streak to 0/5, and restart from Session 1 after merge. Do not guess a cause.

**Session 5 — referent drift and evening correlation.** Run the final full session at a controlled Pacific evening timestamp whose UTC date is the following day. Complete one pressing exercise earlier in the workout. Later, create a fresh substitution proposal for a different current lift, then ask *"How much should I lift?"*. Require: the answer uses the fresh pending proposal; it does not drift back to the earlier completed press; the response carries only engine-owned prescription facts; no model-authored number or exercise identity appears. Complete the replacement and the full workout, and require the exact canonical substitution outcome, correct remaining-work state, preview → approve → write, valid closeout, no duplicate writes, and no cross-session contamination. Then run the corrected review tool with the exact Flight Recorder or session identity and require: the Pacific-local workout date correlates correctly despite the next-day UTC timestamp; `Log_Cleaned`, `Effort` if supplied, `Session_Plans`, and `Session_Plan_Sets` are attributed to this session only; text and JSON verdicts agree; no all-`UNKNOWN` false verdict; no rows from another session. Call `/api/summary/weekly` and require a successful response.

### Common scorecard

Every session proves: (1) clean source `main` and the exact SHA recorded; (2) fresh synthetic identities; (3) correct model-up and provider posture; (4) the exact sandbox and production non-contact; (5) no ambient workbook inheritance; (6) real browser and built client; (7) the full production parser/router/session/coach/validator path; (8) the expected visible conversation; (9) no unsupported mutation or write claim; (10) canonical plan state agrees with the visible wording; (11) exact `Session_Plans` events; (12) exact `Session_Plan_Sets` binding and seal behavior; (13) exact `Log_Cleaned` rows; (14) `Effort` behavior matching whether effort was supplied; (15) a preview before the write; (16) one visible approval; (17) a repeated approval writes nothing; (18) correct closeout state; (19) `InteractionTrace` and turn-write proof join on the live write's `turn_id`; (20) the corrected review tool finds the exact session; (21) `/api/summary/weekly` succeeds; (22) no foreign identity or cross-session rows; (23) no secret, token, fingerprint, or workbook ID leakage; (24) bounded local artifacts with SHA-256 hashes; (25) explicit PASS/FAIL/ERROR/N/A counts and reasons.

**No authorized `N/A` may hide a seam this rehearsal is intended to test.**

### Counting and failure rule

The rehearsal is five consecutive clean passes: 0/5 → 1/5 → 2/5 → 3/5 → 4/5 → 5/5. It does not modify Stage A or Stage B counts.

**Before the first synthetic athlete turn** — stale `main`, a missing credential, provider refusal, sandbox refusal, build or start failure, or instrumentation refusal — the result is **NOT STARTED**. It earns no pass and resets no earned streak. Resolve it and attempt the same numbered session.

**After the first synthetic athlete turn**, any meaningful product, evidence, or adjudication failure means: the session **FAILS**; the rehearsal streak resets to 0/5; Stage B stays 0/5; preserve the evidence; classify the defect; fix one cause in one focused PR; merge the exact passing head; rerun the affected deterministic and browser gates; refresh `main`; restart the rehearsal at Session 1.

A meaningful failure includes incorrect visible behavior, an unsupported state or write claim, wrong routing that changes the result, incorrect canonical state, missing or duplicated or contradictory ledger events, incorrect remaining-work state, a write or seal or closeout failure, duplicate durable rows, cross-session contamination, a model-invented exercise identity or number, a reviewer or adjudicator failure that makes the verdict unreliable, a production-path exception, a scenario expectation that cannot be proven, and fixture-only or false-green test evidence.

**Do not silently retry a failed session and call the retry the same attempt. Never resume at Session 2, 3, 4, or 5 after a reset. Never erase a failed attempt by rerunning it under the same identity. Do not explain away a mismatch because the final write happened.** Visible wording, canonical state, durable ledger, closeout, and review evidence must all agree.

### Autonomous defect-repair loop (governing)

Claude is authorized to investigate, fix, test, review, merge, and verify every genuine defect this rehearsal exposes, except decisions reserved for Dale. **The goal is not five attempts. The goal is five consecutive clean passes on the current product after every discovered defect is fixed.**

Do not stop merely because a session failed, a defect was found, a PR was opened or merged, the streak reset, one session passed, or the implementation session is long. **Repository state is the handoff.** Continue until 5/5 or a genuine owner-reserved decision blocks progress.

**In scope** are verified defects exposed by the five scenarios, the browser execution path, sandbox isolation, preview → approve → write, canonical plan state, substitution truth, visible coaching claims, closeout and ledger verification, trace and write-proof joining, `atlas:review-live` adjudication, `/api/summary/weekly`, the test machinery required for an honest verdict, and the deployment behavior required to validate the final result. **Do not expand into unrelated corpus polish or findings the rehearsal did not exercise.** A newly discovered adjacent defect takes one disposition: FIX NOW, REJECT, RECORD AS DEFERRED, or OWNER DECISION REQUIRED. **Do not silently broaden a PR.**

**After any meaningful failure**, automatically: preserve the complete failed evidence; record the reset to 0/5; identify the first causal defect; classify it as local, authority, missing capability, tooling/adjudication, or environment/deployment; identify the current live authority, the intended sole authority, the competing authority to remove, any unavoidable bridge, its exact sunset, and the net complexity change; branch from refreshed `main`; fix one root cause; add a deterministic regression test that reproduces the failure; add a bite proof showing that test fails when the fix is removed; run every applicable unit, integration, browser, safety, syntax, lint, secret, authority, and drift check; obtain exact-head adversarial review; address every real in-scope finding; merge the exact passing head; refresh `main` and verify a clean worktree with no open PR; verify deployment and Render health when deployed application code changed; restart the rehearsal from Session 1 with fresh synthetic identities.

**Authority defects** take the standing rule: select one winner, route every consumer through it, delete the losing production authority, and add no permanent reconciliation around competing truths. A passing test, import, contract, shadow, or documentation statement does not prove sole live ownership. Every compatibility bridge carries an exact removal condition.

**Stop only** when resolution requires Dale to decide or authorize: production data deletion or rewriting; Sheet schema or migration changes; security or credential changes; provider, model, or runtime selection; production-write authorization; Constitution or invariant changes; phase progression; or a genuinely non-derivable coaching or product-direction choice. **Do not label an ordinary implementation choice owner-reserved merely to stop.**

### PR and execution discipline

Do not stack branches. Use one focused PR for the review-tool repair. Use one focused PR for any product defect the rehearsal discovers. Evidence and status-only PRs may record passed sessions. Do not merge or deploy while an owner workout is occurring or awaiting a verdict. Do not begin Stage B owner testing during this rehearsal. Do not change production flags. Do not touch Dale's existing test rows. Do not alter Sheet schemas. Do not begin Phase 5. Do not fix unrelated corpus findings.

### At 5/5
